| R185 | Medium | Picking a line of a tight item that held code or a table (`- Install`, `  with npm:`, a fence) wrote the name apart after the fence, which made the whole list loose, and a second pick gave the item two names | Fixed `93a0b9f7` | A plain line inside an item took the paragraph path and walked into the code | Every line of an item's first run names the item, on its last line of words before a table, quote or rule; no blank line is written before a following item | `links.test.ts` "a line of an item over code or a table names the item and keeps the list tight" (failed before) |
| R186 | Medium | A fence left open at the end of a note, or closed only by its quote or list ending, got the name written inside the code, both by naming from search and by Copy link | Fixed `93a0b9f7` | Neither checked that the code run's fence ever closed | A block holding a fence that never closes takes no name | `links.test.ts` "code a fence never closes takes no name" (failed before) |
| R187 | Medium | Since R174, a path whose last part held a digit, dot or dash (`/notes/2026 line:/TODO/`, `/tmp/a.txt OR /plan/i`) still took everything up to a later slash as one pattern | Fixed `7cf493c8` | The early stop only fired where letters followed the inner slash | A pattern stops being one at a space after an inner slash that did not close it, or at a slash right after a space | `query.test.ts` "a path whose last part is not letters keeps the terms after it" (failed before) |
| R188 | Medium | A `)` ended a bare word even outside any group, so Replace all of `print()` with `log()` wrote `log())` and changed `print(x)` too | Fixed `7cf493c8` | The parser broke a word at any `)` | A `)` ends a word only inside a group; a lone `)` outside one is still dropped | `query.test.ts` "is part of the word it is written in"; `apply.test.ts` "replaces a word with brackets in it as typed" (failed before) |
| R189 | Medium | Since R188, a `)` right after an empty scope or a lone `-` inside a group was dropped as a stray, so the group never closed: `(task-todo: OR task-done:) plan` matched any open task, and `-(task:) plan` matched notes without "plan" (a round 31 regression) | Fixed `b21d02bd` | The stray-bracket check ran at any depth, reached through a scope or `-` before the group saw its `)` | A `)` is a stray only outside every group | `query.test.ts` "closes after an empty scope or a lone minus" (failed before) |
| R190 | Medium | Since R187, a pattern ending in a space (`/^- /`, `/^#+ /`, `/TODO: /`) was read as two words and found nothing (a round 31 regression) | Fixed `b21d02bd` | The stop at a slash right after a space also refused the closing slash | Only a slash after a space that does not close the pattern ends it | `query.test.ts` "a pattern may end in a space" (failed before) |
| R191 | Medium | Since R182, picking a paragraph right over a quote or callout (`Before you start:` then `> [!warning] ...`) wrote the name at the end of the callout, so its embed and an assistant edit took only the callout; the block list had no entry for the callout (a round 31 regression) | Fixed `19b47eaa` | Naming walked from the paragraph into the quote, and the block span then took the quote alone | Naming, the block span and the block list end a paragraph where a quote starts, as Markdown does | `links.test.ts` "the paragraph and the callout under it are two blocks" (failed before) |
| R192 | Medium | Since R185, a name written apart right before a step numbered past one (`2. Run:` after a top-level fence) had no blank line after it, so the editor and other apps read the step as plain text | Fixed `19b47eaa` | The blank line before a following list item was dropped everywhere, though only a list starting at 1 can follow a paragraph line | The blank line is dropped only where the name sits inside the list the next item continues | `links.test.ts` "a name written apart before a step numbered past one keeps the step a list item" (failed before) |
| R193 | Medium | Since R188, a word with brackets inside a group closed the group early, and the group's own `)` then stuck to its last word: `(f(x) notes)` searched `f(x` and `notes)` and found nothing, and `(print() OR log())` missed `log()` (a round 31 regression) | Fixed `3a328d42` | Inside a group any `)` ended a word, even one the word opened | Inside a group a `)` ends a word only when the word holds no open `(`; a field that reads nothing (`case:`, an empty scope) leaves the next `)` alone | `query.test.ts` "keeps its brackets and leaves the group whole", "a field that gives nothing leaves no bracket behind" (failed before) |
| R194 | Medium | Since R191, a comment, `<pre>` or maths block holding a line that starts with `>` embedded only from that line on, and an assistant delete left the comment open so it hid the rest of the note (a round 32 regression) | Fixed `0e5887bf` | Quotes were read without regard to held blocks, so the `>` line opened a quote that ran to the closing line | Rows of a held block are never quoted, and a held block ends any quote before it | `links.test.ts` "a comment holding a line that starts with > is one block" (failed before) |
| R195 | High | Code, a table or HTML straight under a heading (`## Install` then a fence) embedded with the heading, so an assistant replace or delete of the code removed the heading; a column-0 fence right under a step embedded with the step | Fixed `0e5887bf` | The walk up from a name written apart crossed every written line, headings and list items included (the R149 class, older than round 29) | The walk stops below a heading, and below a list item when it would cross up from code | `links.test.ts` "code straight under a heading or a step is named without them" (failed before) |
| R196 | Medium | Picking a quote with a comment straight under it wrote the name inside the comment, and Copy link's embed took the comment's opening line, so a replace exposed the rest of the comment | Fixed `0e5887bf` | A comment opener was read as a lazy line of the quote | The same change as R194: a held block ends the quote | `links.test.ts` "a comment straight under a quote is not part of it" (failed before) |
| R197 | Medium | Since R193, a `-` or a scope right before `case:` was dropped: `meeting -case:Plan` searched for Plan, and Replace all rewrote the very word excluded; `task:case:Plan` matched lines that are no tasks (a round 33 regression) | Fixed `981eb1b6` | `case:` read nothing itself, and since R193 the word after it was no longer the operand | `case:` takes the term after it as its operand, and gives nothing only before a space, a `)` or the end | `query.test.ts` "case: before a word still takes a minus or a scope" (failed before) |
| R198 | Medium | Since R195, a `^x` written under a list whose item holds code right under its marker line embedded only the code (or half the list), so an assistant edit of it left the list behind; a heading inside an item cut the same way (a round 33 regression) | Fixed `fa25e316` | The new stops in the walk up fired for code and headings that belong to a list item | The walk stops below code or a heading only where it is not part of a list item | `links.test.ts` "a name written under a list whose item holds code is the whole list" (failed before) |
| R199 | Medium | Code right under a line of words (`Run this:` then a fence) was named and embedded together with the words, so an assistant replace or delete of the code also removed `Run this:`; the editor shows them as two blocks | Fixed `fa25e316` | Naming walked from the words into the code, and the walk up from a name written apart crossed from code into the words (older than round 29) | Words and the code right under them are two blocks for naming, embeds and assistant edits; a `^x` written straight under such a fence still names both, as before | `links.test.ts` "code right under words is a block of its own" (failed before); "words over a fence" and the R177 case now name the words alone |
| R200 | Medium | Since R199, the block list still showed words and the code right under them as one entry, so the `[[^^` picker labelled the code's name with the words and an assistant edit aimed at the words rewrote the code (a round 34 regression) | Fixed `34359497` | The block list only started an entry at headings, list lines, held blocks and quotes | An entry also starts where code begins under words, or words begin under code, outside a list item | `links.test.ts` "the block list gives code right under words an entry of its own" (failed before) |
| R201 | Medium | A table or paragraph right under a closing fence, named apart, embedded the code too, and an assistant delete removed it | Fixed `34359497` | The walk up stopped crossing from code into words, but not from words into code | It stops in both directions outside a list item | `links.test.ts` "a table right under code is a block of its own" (failed before) |
| R202 | Medium | Naming a list item whose words end in an indented heading (`1. Install`, `   ## Notes`) wrote the name on the heading, so its embed and assistant edits were the heading alone, while Copy link named the item | Fixed `34359497` | Item naming walked on into the heading line | Item naming stops before a heading | `links.test.ts` "naming an item whose words end in a heading names the item" (failed before) |
| R203 | Medium | A `^x` under a list whose last item ends in a nested quote embedded only the quote, and an assistant delete left `- a` (a round 32 regression of R191) | Fixed `34359497` | The name under a quote took the quote without asking whether it is in an item | A quote inside an item gives way to the item's list | `links.test.ts` "a name under a list ending in a nested quote is the list" (failed before) |
| R204 | Medium | A `^x` under a list that starts right under a paragraph (`Steps:` then `1. a`) embedded the paragraph too, and an assistant delete removed it | Fixed `34359497` | The walk up crossed from a list's first marker line into the words above (older than round 29) | The walk stops at a list's first line when words outside any item sit above it | `links.test.ts` "a name under a list right under words leaves the words out" (failed before) |

**Differential note.** The desktop regex engine was compared with JavaScript (the web's `u`-then-loose compile) on random patterns built from look-arounds, backreferences, named groups, code escapes, braces, classes, anchors and repeats, over ASCII, accented, astral and CRLF subjects, with and without folding. An early generator repeated patterns, so its counts are not claimed. After the fix: four runs of 20,000 cases (about 14,650 distinct patterns each), 20,266 cases compiled on both sides, and 6 differed, all a pattern JavaScript only took in loose mode next to a character outside the basic plane, or an empty match next to one. A separate auditor run of 40,000 patterns (32,000 distinct, 8,544 compiled on both) found only the `.` and `\r` difference, since fixed (R30).

### Search, links and tags, accepted and reported

- **Low, accepted:** the properties table shows a block-list item's trailing comment as part of its chip (L9; the list editor relies on the raw item to write the comment back); links inside an inline code span that runs over several lines are indexed (L10); renaming a note to a name holding `#`, `^`, `[`, `]` or `|` writes a markdown link, so an embed of it becomes a plain link (L11; no wikilink can hold such a name); desktop refuses `\p{…}`, legacy octal escapes, identity escapes of letters and quantified look-aheads, which only the web's loose fallback reads; desktop `\w` and case folding do not take `ſ`, `ς`, the Kelvin sign or the Ohm sign, as JavaScript's `iu` does; a checkbox on a found line inside a code fence is still drawn, though it no longer ticks. Desktop `[A-Z]` with case folding takes Turkish `ı`, and `ẞ` and `Ⱥ` fold only on the web; with two keys that differ only in case the web and desktop read different ones; an alias row in link completion writes `[[Alias]]` even when another note has that name; a loose-mode pattern or an empty match beside a character outside the basic plane can land differently (see the differential note); look-arounds copy their captures uncharged, so a pattern with very many groups is slow; replace-all ignores `$<name>` and `$10`; `[]` and `[^]` read differently on desktop; a byte order mark before an opening `#tag` makes a tag only on the web; a block named while typing continues after the caret gets no link; picking a completion inside `[[x|alias]]` under a markdown link setting drops the alias.
- **Low, accepted (round 4):** patterns not led by a plain run whose cost still grows with the square of a line, such as `(?:.(?!TODO))*bug` or `a.*b.*c`, can still stop early on desktop on lines of several hundred characters; a key written inside an unindented continuation of a multi-line quoted or flow value is read on the web but not on desktop; a task line after a byte order mark on the first line is drawn with a checkbox that does not tick; desktop refuses `a{1,65536}`, a 2000-word alternation, `(?i:a)` and a repeated group name, which the web takes; desktop takes `[\u{41}-\u{43}]` with a loose-mode pattern that the web refuses, so its hits cannot be replaced; the Angstrom sign and other letters whose lower case has another UTF-8 length fold differently (as the Kelvin sign does); the "absolute" link setting writes no leading slash, so a namesake at the same relative path can win.
- **Low, accepted (round 5):** a folded single-quoted front matter value whose link sits on a continuation line, and a block scalar line that starts with a quote, can be requoted wrongly on rename; in a flow list the list's `[` can open a markdown label across two items; `LONGEST_LINK` counts UTF-16 units on the web and characters on desktop; `\\[[Plan]]` reads as no link on both sides; code-span blanking in a label differs per emoji; counted groups around a run such as `(.*,){5}x` passed the cost judge (since fixed, R40); U+2028, U+2029, U+0085 and a lone BOM line read differently on web and desktop; removing a tag can leave a lone `-` or `()`, and removing the first item of a plain `tags: a, "b"` leaves a value that starts with a quote; `#Work` and `#work` in one note count twice in the tag tree; `"a" b: x` is a key only on the web; very long non-ASCII keys are cut at different lengths; an overlapping phrase that starts at a task box can be missed on the web.
- **Low, accepted (round 6):** `tags: work's` reads as two tags; an alternation led by a run whose other branch shares a common letter (`.*TODO|the`) can still spend the desktop budget on lines of thousands of characters; an alias rewrite of `[[Bob#H]]` shows "Bob" rather than "Bob > H", and a new name that needs a markdown link drops the shown words.
- **Low, accepted (round 7):** undo of a rename runs the link rewrite only when the rename rewrote something, and does not restore the link text it changed (the links still reach their notes); `inTableRow` takes any line with a `|` as a table row, so an alias rewrite there writes `\|` (read the same way); renaming a tag to `null`, `true` or `1e3` writes a value YAML reads as another type; replace-all under the loose regex reading (`\#(.)`) can still split an emoji; ticking a scratchpad task from search while the scratchpad is open is written over by the pad's next save; `[d:"a b"]` keeps the quotes; regex flags other than `i` are dropped; astral against high BMP letters sort differently on web and desktop; fuzzy close matches differ on astral letters and lines past the 400-letter window; desktop `search_space` is not cancelled when a newer search starts (bounded by its budget); a single run that can cross lines after one letter (`e[\s\S]*Q`) still costs about the note length squared (250 ms on 20 KB).
- **Low, accepted (round 8):** every rename reads every canvas and pages file in the space to find card links (fast enough at a few hundred); a note whose name has a backtick loses its wikilinks, since a code span wins; clicking a link before the first index pass ends can make a second note of that name; six note-wide `[^x]+x` patterns with nothing in front are refused; a flow list `[[[Plan]], [[Other]]]` is not requoted on rename into an odd name; a file whose name starts with `word:` (only made outside Orden) gets a markdown link read as a web address.
- **Low, accepted (round 9):** "Copy link" on a block or a link writes the bare name, which can reach a namesake where it is pasted; a canvas file card written outside Orden with another case or Unicode form counts as a backlink but is not moved on rename; Split or Extract making `idea.md` beside `Idea.md` on a case-sensitive disk links the older one; the judge weighs each look-ahead as if it multiplied the rest, so `(?=.*foo)(?=.*bar)` is refused; it refused `(\w+,\s*)+\w+` (since fixed, R72); the graph filter judges with `u` but compiles with `iu`; a negated refused pattern (`-/(a+)+b/`) matches every note; `[n:>4]` misses `1e3`, `+5`, `.5`; `n: ""` counts as null.
- **Low, accepted (round 10):** Split and Extract do not retarget links to the headings and blocks they move (`[[Plan#Later]]` keeps pointing into Plan), a missing feature rather than a break; a rename that puts `: ` or ` #` into a plain value running over several lines leaves YAML that cannot be read; a merge run before the first index pass ends pastes links unrebased; a dotted name such as `Version 2.0` gets no `.md` in markdown links Orden writes (Orden reads them; other apps may not); an Extract alias holding `|` in a table row is not escaped.
- **Low, accepted (round 10 search):** the judge counts any class that takes no `a` as a word-long run, so `[\d\s]+kg` or `(\d+\W+)+kg` take about half a second on a note made only of numbers; `(#\w+\s*)+\.` is slow only on hundreds of kilobytes of nothing but tags; the letter-apart check probes ASCII and the pattern's own letters only, so `(?:\p{Lu}+\p{Greek}+)+$` counts as apart (not slow in practice); replace-all does not join words around a `case:` partway through, replaces a scope's own word (`plan section:Budget`), and leaves alone words the note splits with two spaces or a line; front matter block scalars, values continued or written on the next indented line and nested flow lists read only in part (Orden writes one-line values).
- **Low, accepted (round 11):** a rename into `@Alice`, `'x` or `x #y` breaks a plain front matter link followed by a comment (`up: [[Plan]] # parent`) or inside a list of maps; Split and Extract name notes with markup left in (`## Plan ##`, `- [ ] Buy milk`, a heading holding `[[Bob]]`); a merge rewrites `tags: a, b` as a flow list Orden still reads the same, moves a joined list key to the end and drops its comment lines, and mixes CRLF and LF; replace all does not join a word with a group after it (`quarterly (plan OR budget)`) and replaces both a regex and a word beside it (`/\d+/ items`), which may be meant.
- **Low, accepted (round 12):** `(?:\t[^\t]*){11}` (tab-separated columns written with `\t`) is refused while a literal tab runs; a look-ahead holding a whole repeat (`(?=(?:\S+\s)+\S+)`) or a tail of `^` after one is weighed as if nothing followed (about 0.5 to 1 s on 20 KB, and the first never finds anything); a merge stops copying a block list at a comment at the start of a line, so later items are lost; a quoted phrase followed by a word (`"quarterly plan" budget`) is joined into one phrase and replaces nothing.
- **Low, accepted (round 13):** a markdown link picked into a front matter flow list or before a trailing comment is not quoted, and a name with `'` picked into a single-quoted value is not doubled; after an escaped backtick right before a backtick run, the index reads a code span the editor does not show; the 60-letter name cut can split a flag or drop a combining accent; the judge reads `(?i:…)` as a plain group, so `(?:(?i:[a-z]+)\s)+[a-z]+` runs (desktop refuses `(?i:` anyway); it still refuses some fast forms: `\| *([^|]*?) *\|`, `\b(?:\w+\s)+\w+\b`, `(?:\w+, )*\w+ and \w+`, lists of wikilinks, `(?:#{1,6} .*\n)+`, `(?:\d{1,3},)+\d{3}`.
- **Low, accepted (round 14):** a group that wraps a repeat in `?` or an alternation, followed by `\b`, `\B` or `^`, is weighed as if nothing followed (`(?:.*(?:\n.*)+\n)?\b`, 1.4 s on 20 KB); the judge now refuses some fast negative look-behinds with a run that can cross the note (`(?<!\[\[[^\]]*)\d+`); replace all writes twice with `section:quarterly plan`, `block:quarterly plan` and a scope typed twice (`line:quarterly line:plan`), rewrites a front matter value with `quarterly content:plan`, and replaces nothing with `plan quarterly plan`; a web page kept before R82 under a dashless name can be kept again as a second shortcut; Split at a `---` rule makes a note whose first part reads as front matter; a backtick name or heading picked into a double-quoted front matter value writes `\``, which YAML refuses.
- **Low, accepted (round 15):** a setext title loses to a later `##` heading; front matter closing past line 40 names the note after its first key; a BOM before `# Title` hides it; a comment-only `---` block is read as words, as Orden does not take it as front matter; `<img src='x'>`, unquoted `src=`, `srcset` and numeric entities in trusted raw HTML are not resolved in Reading view, and `![](a&amp;b.png)` is not decoded; the judge takes a lead of two letters as rare, so a common word followed by a line run before a look-behind holding a line run (`\bthe\b.*(?<=TODO.*)`, 0.3 s on 20 KB and 3 s on 200 KB of long paragraphs) passes, as `the[\s\S]*TODO` does on 200 KB; it refuses fast look-arounds whose look-behind leads or whose look-ahead closes a run (`(?<=\[\[)[^\]|]+(?=\]\])`, `(?<=")[^"]*(?=")`).
- **Low, accepted (round 16):** a fence opened on a list marker or quote line (`` - ```bash ``, `` > ```bash ``) is not seen when taking a title; a draft starting with a tag line is named after its tags; `%% # hidden %%` and `$$` blocks are read as headings for a title.
- **Low, accepted (round 17):** `(?:.*\n){0,3}.*TODO` and up to four lines before a match still pass the judge (225 ms on 200 KB of prose, up to 3.9 s on 200 KB of 1,000-character table rows); "Move up to projectx" shows the folded parent name; `istanbul` does not find `İstanbul`; the desktop warm search cache is keyed by modification time and length, so an edit of the same length within one timestamp tick on a coarse disk can leave stale text; removing a tag can leave `[x, ]` or a doubled `[job, job]`; a note that starts with a picture is named after the link; on a case-sensitive disk `![](pic.png)` beside `b/Pic.png` shows another folder's `pic.png`; a relative `typora-root-url` is read from the top of the space; a plain-text card cover draws a broken picture; raw HTML `<img>` inside an embedded note is still looked up from the host note.
- **Low, accepted (round 18):** a wikilink cover (`cover: "[[image.png]]"`) is looked up as a markdown picture, so it prefers the file beside the note; `[[#Heading]]` inside an embedded note jumps within the host page.
- **Low, accepted (round 18 search):** `"tags": [a]`, `'tags': [a]` and `tags : [a]` are properties but not tags, and `tags:[a]` is tags but no property; the judge still refuses some fast table and CSV forms (`(?:.*?\|){5}`, `\|.*\|.*QZX`, `,.*,.*QZX`, `.*:\s*$`, `(?:\n\s*){3,}`); line-run patterns such as `(?:.*\n){0,8}TODO` still pass at about 1.5 s on 200 KB of 1,000-character lines (15 to 200 ms on prose); the web search worker is never stopped, so a pattern that slips past the judge holds later searches.
- **Low, accepted (round 19):** a Reading-view link to a note whose file name holds `#` or a literal `%41` opens the wrong path (the href keeps `#` and `%` as written).
- **Low, accepted (round 19 search):** a run that ends a look-ahead inside an optional group is weighed as free (`([^,\n]+(?=[^.]*))?\0`, a fuzzer find, 5.9 s on 20 KB of tabs); `\W+$` is now refused, though it is only slow on notes in other scripts.
- **Low, accepted (round 20 search):** `\S+\W+X`, `\S*\W*X9` and `\p{L}+\W*X` still pass the judge and are slow only on long runs of Chinese without spaces; `\W+\w+` is now refused, though it is only slow on notes in other scripts; fast alternations under a repeat are refused (`(?:\w|-)+`, `(?:TODO|FIXME)+:`).
- **Low, accepted (round 21):** TextBundle and Markdown-with-pictures copy a cover picture into the bundle but leave `cover:` naming the old place; a note embed in a bundle links to the note's own folder, which the bundle does not hold.
- **Low, accepted (round 21 search):** in a case-sensitive search the judge does not see `(?i:…)` when telling sets apart (`(?:e(?i:[A-Z ]+))+X`, a made-up form); runs with no space on Chinese text still pass (`\S*[^\s]*?!`, 1.2 s on 20 KB); stacked ranged repeats of overlapping classes (`(?:.{0,5}.{1,3}){3}QZ`, a fuzzer shape) take 0.4 to 1.3 s on 20 KB.
- **Low, accepted (round 22):** a reference-style picture definition (`[logo]: shot.png`) is not rewritten for Markdown export, and in bundles a picture named both ways is copied twice (it still shows); a definition on a CRLF line is not rewritten in a bundle (open notes reach the exporter with LF); copying an embedded note's footnotes out of Reading view may not turn them back into `[^1]`, since their ids now carry the embed's prefix (read, not run).
- **Low, accepted (round 22 links):** a merge relinks `[[From#Goals]]` to the first `## Goals` of the merged note, which can be the target's own (a heading link names the first heading of that text; Obsidian does the same); a rename that writes the new title leaves `[[Plan#Plan]]` as `[[Road#Plan]]` until it is undone; extracting a whole list item takes its bullet into the new note and leaves the link as a bare line; removing a tag alone on a line inside a paragraph leaves an empty line; `[[Note #work]]` is read as a tag; merging front matter into an empty note leaves a `---` rule after it.
- **Low, accepted (round 22 search):** a `\b` or `\B` after a pattern's last letter keeps it weighed as ending there (`[\d\s]+\s\B`, 0.7 s on 20 KB of TSV, a made-up form); `\t[^\t]*\t` and the Russian money form `\d+(?:\s?\d{3})*(?:,\d+)?\s?₽` are refused though fast; a note the judge still misjudges now costs 3 s and its own hits on the web, not the search (R123).
- **Low, accepted (round 23):** Split, Extract and rename in a space other than the active one do not see that a new name would take over a link to a namesake (R128 covers the active space); `（\W+X` and `“\W+X` still pass the judge (1.7 s on 200 KB of Chinese), since paired openers are kept rare so that `“[^”]*”` and `（[^）]*）` run, as `"[^"]*"` does; near a line over 2,000 letters, `^`, `$`, look-behinds and matches across lines change, since each long line is searched on its own (`/^\{/` finds a long JSON line in the middle of a note).
- **Low, accepted (round 24):** `\d+(?:,\d{3})*(?:\.\d+)?` is refused though fast (the round 13 thousands class); `(?:.|\n){0,200}plan` is refused, where `[\s\S]{0,200}plan` runs; desktop still tries every start before a counted group, so CSV column patterns over long prose lines can run out of steps (the round 4 class); a note written during a cold web walk whose path sorts before the walk's place can be left out of the space until the next fill (read, not seen).
- **Low, accepted (round 25 links):** `blocksOf` lists front matter list items as blocks, and picking one names nothing; a first paragraph right after front matter with no blank line cannot be picked as a block; a line starting `2024. ` reads as a list item for block naming; a `^id` on its own line after a blank line under a list is labelled with the last item, while its embed shows the whole list; `[[TODO]]` reaches a file named `TODO` with no extension in the Links panel; tags inside `<!-- -->`, `%% %%` and `$$` are read and renamed (the same on web and desktop); undoing a rename that wrote the title misses CRLF notes; a heading written by hand with a trailing `^id` keeps the id in its name.
- **Low, accepted (round 25 search):** since R133, `:\s*(.*)$` and `-\s*(.*)$` are refused though fast (the round 18 `.*:\s*$` class); a note deleted while a web walk awaits a fetch can shift the walk by one note; desktop counted groups such as `(?:\S+\s){1,4}QZX` still run out of steps on long Chinese text without spaces (the round 20 class).
- **Low, accepted (round 26 links):** a table, quote or sub-list nested in a list item gets its name written there, which Orden still reads; an existing name after a sub-item can leave an item with two names; a `^x` line straight under a list (no blank line) names the whole list, labelled by its first item; markdown links inside an embed in Reading view reach notes only.
- **Low, accepted (round 26 search):** on the web, `.{0,100}.{0,100}QZX` and `.{0,100} .{0,100}QZX` pass the judge and take 0.75 to 7 s on 200 KB (made-up forms; the 3 s worker restart caps them, and desktop now runs them in milliseconds); a lead a note is full of followed by a run that crosses lines and whose stop sign the note lacks (`- \[ \] [^"]*"` on a task list) can take 1 to 3 s on 200 KB, as before round 25.
- **Low, accepted (round 27 links):** a `^c` on an unindented continuation line of a list item (`- item one\ncontinued here ^c`) embeds as that line only, and picking the item names its first line; a merge of two notes that hold the same block id makes `[[From#^id]]` reach the target's own block (like the accepted heading merge Low). Since R157, the round 25 Lows about front matter list items in the block list and the first paragraph after front matter are fixed.
- **Low, accepted (round 27 search):** since R153, a lead holding a digit, Cyrillic letter or Han character other than the judge's one sample per script (`\| 1[^\]]+\]\]`, `，我[^\]]+\]\]`) passes and takes up to 0.5 s on 200 KB (the round 26 class); desktop finds a third of the hits for `(?:\S+\s+){0,5}meeting` in a 200 KB CSV (the round 24 counted group class). Round 26's fuzz generators repeated after a few hundred cases; round 27 replaced them and found 0 differences in 163,000 cases.
- **Low, accepted (round 28 links):** a name on a closing fence inside a list item, or on a fence under a paragraph, is labelled in the block list by the item or paragraph while its embed is the code; a deep fence named on its closing line embeds with its indent; a fence name reaches up through two fences that touch; a hand-written `^x` straight after front matter takes the front matter; `blockIds` reads `^id` on front matter lines; a found code line ending like `x ^2` is offered as a block name that reaches nothing; removing the only tag of `## #work` leaves `##`.
- **Low, accepted (round 29 links):** the block list shows the code of a loose list item as an entry of its own, while picking it names the item (the second pick finds the name); the editor's "Copy link" on maths inside a list item was not re-checked (the editor reads its own syntax tree). Fixed alongside R168 to R172: pairing openers was quadratic on notes with many unclosed `$$`, openers indented four spaces or more were not held, and any line holding `$$` closed maths.
- **Low, accepted (round 29 search):** a path whose last part is only flag letters (`/notes/is`) is still a pattern; on a note-wide match, a line holding only a byte order mark after the first line shows as an empty row on desktop; `[date:>2025]` compares a bare year as text, so every date in 2025 counts as after it (web and desktop agree); typed backslashes escape the next letter, so `C:\Users\name` searches without them (search from a selection quotes it); Replace on a note edited since the search looks for each changed hit over every line, slow on large notes.
- **Low, accepted (round 30 links):** in a CRLF note with no final line break, a name written apart under the last line uses LF; an unclosed `$$` or `<!--` can close on a matching line inside a later code fence; the item lookup walks up through a column-0 fence, so indented code after it can name the item above; a maths line starting with `- ` makes the fence reader open a list, so indented code after that maths is not seen as code. Fixed alongside R180: a name written apart after indented code at the end of a note no longer adds a blank line.
- **Low, accepted (round 30 search):** `/^x/m` and `/a.b/s` are read as patterns, but `m` and `s` are ignored (the matcher compiles with `g`, `i` and `u`).
- **Low, accepted (round 31 links):** naming a block apart inside a list nested two or more levels deep (a table or quote in a sub-item) can make Reading view space the outer list as a loose one (22 picks in the round 31 fuzz run of 4,000 notes); a paragraph line ending in `->` is named apart rather than at its end; a spaced rule (`* * *`) reads as a list item for naming; a nested quote in an item can get its name on a bare `>` line. Copy link and naming from search can still put the name on different lines of a list item (both reach the item).
- **Low, accepted (round 31 search):** a group of one word (`(draft)`) searches the word, so Replace all of `(draft)` with `(final)` keeps the brackets around it (grouping is the documented reading); `/a/b c/` is now two words, not one pattern.
- **Low, accepted (round 32 links):** a table or quote followed directly by `$$` maths (no blank line) is one block in the editor's reading of Markdown but two in naming, so naming the table ends the editor's table there; maths straight under a quote counts as part of the quote; two different quotes on touching lines (`> a` then `- > b`) read as one; a table at column 0 straight under a nested sub-item gets its name indented as code (the R181 class); indented code in an item can take in a shallower paragraph after it. Fixed alongside R191: a name written apart under a quote reaches back over the whole quote.
- **Low, accepted (round 33 search):** a `)` outside any group followed straight by text, a quote or `(` stays in its word (`a )(b c)` reads `)(b` and `c)`); `path:)` searches for a path holding `)`; `[up:=[[Plan]]]` falls back to text, and a timestamp with an offset is compared as wall-clock time.
- **Low, accepted (round 33 links):** a hand-written `^x` straight under a quote that sits right under a paragraph (`Para`, `> q`, `^x`) is listed with the paragraph while its embed is both; Copy link on a quote ending in a bare `>` line followed by a paragraph line writes `> ^id`, which pulls that paragraph into the quote.
- **Low, accepted (round 34 links):** a hand-written `^g` straight under a heading embeds nothing; `## Install`, `Some text`, `^x` is listed under the heading's label while its embed is the text; Copy link on a `<textarea>` holding a blank line writes the name inside it, since the editor ends that HTML block at the blank line.
- **Low, accepted (round 35 links):** a heading indented less than its item's content (`1. a`, `  ## H`) is taken as part of the item; with a lazy line in an item (`- a`, `lazy`, indented code), a `^x` under the list embeds only the code (the round 27 lazy-line class).
- **Low, accepted (round 35 search):** `-case: Plan` and `task:case: Plan` (with a space) drop the minus or scope, as `- x` and `task: x` do; suggestions are not offered after `case:` (`case:ta`); a query of more than about 3,000 nested fields (`case:` or `line:` repeated) throws.
- **Reported:** N16, a note's `file.size` becomes the UTF-16 length after an in-app save (`rows/src/store.ts`), is outside this area and went to the coordinator.

## Security

Security pass of 2026-11-15, branch `claude/project-thread-ewdevv` from `unstable`. Area: rendering of untrusted content and the webview's reach into IPC, the sync server's sign-in, sessions, OAuth, sharing, CSRF/CORS and logs, remote machines and shared terminals, Pass's end-to-end trust, and the published advisories of the JS and Rust dependencies (`pnpm audit`, `cargo audit` on both Cargo locks). Rows are numbered SEC1 and on. Each fix has a test that failed before it unless the row says otherwise; the touched suites, `tsc`, eslint and prettier ran again on Node 24.

The Rust Tauri commands themselves were the desktop backend thread's (#2139) and the server's correctness the sync thread's (#2140); findings that landed in their code are noted. An agent's token reading protected notes over REST is #2140's S11 and is not repeated here.

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SEC1 | High | A malicious or compromised account could hand a Pass vault a key it knows, and the devices took it and sealed every later item under it | Fixed `b58a9902` | `vaultDistrust` read the owner pin only from the row the account sent, so a row with `accepted: null` fell to the unpinned path, where any signer vouches for itself; a key at the same version with none held here was taken with no proof; and each pull stored the row's (stripped) pin over the device's | The pin is the device's own (`keptPin`), which a row cannot drop; a same-version key with nothing held needs the owner's proof | `vault-trust.test.ts` (forged key with and without the pin, same-version swap; 4 failed before); pass-sync 289 passed |
| SEC2 | Medium | Taking a member out of a Pass vault wrapped the new key to them again when the account kept listing them | Fixed `4355b739` | `wrappedToEveryone` wraps to whoever `members()` returns | The rotation of a removal skips the address it took out | `pass-share-flow.test.ts` "wraps the new key to nobody it took out" (failed before); pass flows 167 passed |
| SEC3 | Medium | "End every other session" left apps connected over OAuth connected, and their refresh tokens never run out, so the somebody the security mail warns of kept read and write access | Fixed `96639a43` | `DELETE /v1/sessions` ended sessions, devices, rooms and machines, never `oauth_grants` | It deletes the account's OAuth grants too | `oauth.test.ts` "ending every other session disconnects every connected client too" (failed before); 68 passed |
| SEC4 | Medium | A space's owner could put their space, with write access, into anybody's account: join their own approval link as a guest typing the victim's address, accept, and the victim's next sign-in claimed it | Fixed `11cf67a3` (approach waits on David's choice) | `claimGuestsAt` treated a guest's self-typed address as proved | Only the guest session the signing-in device holds is claimed, also when that guest follows an invitation to its address | `guests.test.ts` "hands nothing to the account at an address a guest only typed" (failed before); guests, account, share 463 passed |
| SEC5 | Medium | An AI answer's "click to load" hold on remote pictures was skipped for `https:/host/…`, `https:host/…` and (found by the review of the first fix) `http:host/…`, which the page fetches from that host with no click: a prompt injection could leak what it read through the URL | Fixed `b09ea611`, `2d1b6bf8` | `ELSEWHERE` was a regex that wants `//`; the first fix parsed against an `http:` base, which reads `http:host` as relative | A picture is drawn only when it resolves to the page's own place against both a special and a non-special base (or is a drive path) | `drawn.test.ts` 7 spellings (6 failed before one fix or the other); ai-sidebar 119 passed |
| SEC6 | Medium | `within(folder, path)` answered a path that steps out with `..` (`/s/Work/../Secret/x`) as inside the folder; callers use it to say which space a path belongs to, an agent's reach among them (`terminal/src/agent/online.ts`) | Fixed `6bc30d26` | The prefix fast path and the step compare both kept `..` steps | A `..` step after the folder answers null | `within.test.ts` (3 failed before); platform, space-kept, explorer, chats-client, terminal, workspace 2568 passed. The AI chat thread also edits `space-paths.ts` (Windows trailing dots); the hunks do not overlap |
| SEC7 | Medium | Rust HTTPS (sync, AI providers) still trusted the emSign intermediates whose private keys are public (RUSTSEC-2026-0382/0383/0381), plus four rustls advisories | Fixed `90fb4508` | rustls 0.23.45, rustls-webpki 0.103.15, webpki roots 1.0.9 in both Cargo locks | rustls 0.23.46, rustls-webpki 0.103.16, webpki-root-certs and webpki-roots 1.0.10 | `cargo audit` clean of them on both locks; the four crates build (scratch crate); the full crate builds tonight with the suite |
| SEC8 | Medium | Every website could make the browser version write into the reader's notes, add to-dos or run app commands, with no prompt, by sending them to `orden.so/?orden=orden://append?...` | Waiting on David | `startAutomation` follows the query link with no question; the desktop app has the system's prompt in front | Proposed: the existing confirm sheet before a link writes or runs a command | traced, not fixed |
| SEC9 | Medium | Sign-in mail can be stopped for everybody for the rest of the UTC day by about 25 addresses asking for codes (500 new addresses, 900 mails a day, both fail closed) | Waiting on David | Global mail ceilings refuse sign-in codes | Proposed: keep part of the day's mail for accounts that exist | traced, not fixed |
| SEC12 | Medium | A published site's own address answered the whole account API and the OAuth sign-in pages, so the owner's `publish.js` on `name.orden.so` could open Orden's real sign-in and consent pages on its own origin, rewrite them and read the code a visitor typed | Fixed `6e0af0ab` | Only the final `app.all('*')` asked which host a request came to; every route before it answered on any host | `/v1`, `/v2`, `/oauth`, `/.well-known`, `/mcp`, `/rooms` and `/even` answer 404 on a host that is a published site; the app's own hosts and Pass are unchanged | `site-host.test.ts` (5 failed before); full sync suite 3076 passed |
| SEC13 | Low | A device kept its own Pass pin over a newer one the reader signed on another device, so it refused every later key of that vault (found by the review of SEC1) | Fixed `802cf4c4` | `keptPin` preferred the local pin without checking either | The pin is whichever of the row's and the device's the reader signed, the row's first | `vault-trust.test.ts` 2 new cases |
| SEC14 | Low | "End every other session" left OAuth codes not yet exchanged, which a session ended mid-consent could still turn into a grant (review of SEC3) | Fixed `b2c53593` | Only `oauth_grants` were deleted | `oauth_codes` too | `oauth.test.ts` (failed before) |
| SEC15 | Low | A crash report's version or platform could put a link, a mention or terminal escapes into the issue `scripts/crash-reports.mjs` files and the lines it prints | Fixed `bf858bfa` | Versions and platforms were written outside the fence and printed as received | `plain()` keeps letters, digits and a few separators | `crash-tool.test.ts` (failed before) |
| SEC16 | Medium | The SEC12 gate was tested on the raw path, so `/%761/me`, `/%761/auth/code`, `/%762/pass/...`, `/%6fauth/authorize` and `/%6dcp` still reached the account on a published site's host (final re-audit) | Fixed `ce662bd6` | Hono routes on the percent-decoded path; the gate read `new URL(url).pathname` | The gate reads `context.req.path`, the path Hono routes on | `site-host.test.ts` 5 encoded cases (failed before); site, blog and site-host tests 207 passed |
| SEC17 | Medium | A mermaid diagram in an AI answer (`A@{ img: "https://..." }`, an `<img>` label, `url(...)` in a style, `@import` in `themeCSS`) fetched a remote address with no click while it was drawn, the leak SEC5 closed for pictures (final re-audit) | Fixed `1de2ba8f` | The hold ran on the answer's HTML after drawing; mermaid fetches node pictures itself while rendering (`new Image()` in `imageSquare`), and its `<image>` was not held | In answers, a diagram whose source names anything that could reach elsewhere (`//`, `\\`, a network scheme, `img`, `image`, `src`, `href`, `url(`, `@import`, entities, tags other than `<br>`) stays code; `<image>` is stripped like other media | `drawn-diagrams.test.ts` 8 cases (failed before), a plain diagram still drawn; ai-sidebar, chat-kit and chats-client 352 passed. The fetch itself was traced in mermaid 11.17.2, not run |
| SEC18 | High | A password-locked note, folder or space put in Recently deleted came back unlocked when the reader put it back or undid the deletion (reported by the CLI/MCP thread) | Fixed `2782727e` | `Protection.gone` and `forget` dropped the lock keys and nothing gave them back: protection had no `cameBack`, unlike item metadata | Locks that go with a deletion are kept by its trash id and by their place; putting back from the device trash, undoing, or the account putting a note back (`restoredAt`) brings them back, and a space trashed here keeps its locks until it is put back | `protection.test.ts` 4 cases, `trash.test.ts` device, undo, account and space cases (6 failed before); trash, protect, file-ops and workspace 1282 passed. Not covered: a space held by the account and put back from its trash relies on the locks the account still holds |
| SEC19 | Medium | On Windows an agent could read or write any locked note below the top of its space by spelling a folder or name with a trailing dot or space (`Folder./Secret.md`), and a crafted link opened it with no lock screen (final re-audit 3) | Fixed `d84f80cb` | Windows opens `Folder.` as `Folder`, and the crate already treats them as one name, but `sameName` and `pathKeyAs` compared them as two, so no lock covered the path | On Windows, names are compared and keyed as Windows opens them, without trailing dots and spaces | `windows-names.test.ts` 8 spellings (failed before), and a dotted folder on Linux stays its own; platform, protect, agent-host, agent-docs and the Windows suites 947 passed. Not run on Windows |
| SEC20 | Medium | An agent could copy locked notes to a folder it can read by running `export-space` or a note export with a `path`, which skipped every scope and lock check (final re-audit 3) | Fixed `4eeef13f` | `runCommand` gives an export with a path no scopes and asked nothing of protection | An agent's export refuses when the space holds a lock not open to agents, or the note in front is not | `agent-host/src/commands.test.ts` (failed before); agent-host 38 passed |
| SEC21 | Low | The guest and token allowlists read the raw path while Hono routes on the decoded one, so a token reached `/v1/spaces/%61vailable/changes` (it only says whether a subdomain is taken) | Fixed `d11c7a27` | Same pattern as SEC16 | Both read `context.req.path` | `programs.test.ts` (failed before); programs, guests, mcp and oauth 205 passed |
| SEC22 | High | An agent could move a note out of a locked folder and then read it: `move_file Private/Diary.md Diary.md`, `read_note Diary.md` (re-audit 4) | Fixed `a77d0d01` | `Protection.moved` rewrote only the locks at or under the moved path; the folder's lock stayed behind, so nothing covered the note where it landed (`copied` already kept one) | A note or folder moved out from under a lock, in its space or to another, takes that lock with it unless it lands somewhere already locked | `protection.test.ts` 3 cases (2 failed before) |
| SEC23 | High | Undoing a merge wrote the merged-away locked note back with no lock, the reader's undo or an agent's `undo-file` alike (re-audit 4, same class as SEC18) | Fixed `a77d0d01` | The merge's deletion carried no trash id and `unmerge` wrote the note back as a new file, so the buried lock was never asked for | The merge's deletion carries an id kept in the undo, and putting it back hands the note back under it, so its lock and item metadata return | `composing.test.ts`, `undoing.test.ts` (failed before); workspace, file-undo, protect, trash, platform, file-ops 1962 passed |
| SEC24 | Medium | An agent's note export pulled in embedded notes the reader had unlocked but not opened to agents, from any space (re-audit 4) | Fixed `3dd7cff1` | SEC20 checked only the note in front; embeds are read through the reader-level `locked` | An agent's export refuses while any space holds a lock not open to agents, and when the note in front is not | `agent-host/src/commands.test.ts` (failed before) |
| SEC25 | Medium | App commands that write the note in front (`reformat`, `edit.*`, `format.*`, `extract-selection`...) ran for an agent on a note locked to agents (re-audit 4) | Fixed `3dd7cff1` | Those commands needed only `notes.write`; nothing asked protection | A command that writes refuses when the note in front is not open to agents; `protect.*` commands are refused to every agent, autonomous ones too | `agent-host/src/commands.test.ts`; agent-host 41 passed |
| SEC26 | Medium | A renamed or moved note left its earlier versions at its old path, where an agent could restore them into a new note and read the words of a note since locked (re-audit 4) | Fixed `8b51f483` | History is kept by path and only rehomed when the spaces folder moved | Every move or rename rehomes the history of that path; the crate rehomes an exact note path too | `history.rs` `a_history_follows_its_note_when_the_note_is_renamed`; history tests 19 passed |
| SEC27 | High | An agent could delete a locked note (or its locked folder), make a new note at that path and restore the old versions into it, then read them; it could also move a note it may not read into a folder open to agents (re-audit 5) | Fixed `a20786be`, `5d56b364` | `move_file` and `trash_file` checked only that the agent may write there; history is kept by path and stayed at the deleted note's place | An agent may move or delete only what no lock shut to agents covers or holds. Deleting a locked note or folder, by anyone, moves its versions away with its buried lock, and putting it back (trash, undo, the account) brings them back where it lands | `guarded.test.ts` 5 cases, `buried-history.test.ts` 4 cases (new); protect, trash, agent-host 334 passed |
| SEC28 | Medium | A move onto a path that already had versions left the moved note's history behind at its old path (re-audit 5) | Fixed `5d56b364` | `rehome_in` skipped a destination with history of its own | The versions join the folder already there, never over one of the same moment | `history.rs` `a_history_joins_one_already_kept_at_the_new_path`; history tests 20 passed |
| SEC29 | High | A buried lock and its versions came back only under the exact name they were buried with, so a note the account put back from a deleted locked folder, a folder deleted by sync and put back from this device's trash, the undo of a copy or an import, and a space deleted from the account or left all came back unlocked or left their versions at the old place (re-audit 6) | Fixed `1d6e4877` | `unburied` asked one name (the trash id, else the exact place); `forget` buried nothing without a trash id; `gone` dropped the lock before its versions were away | A lookup takes the trash id, then the place, then every folder above that went with its locks, bringing back the locks that cover the note and its versions from where the folder's went; a space buries its locks and versions by place too, and the account putting a space back asks for them; the lock is dropped only once its versions are away | `buried-history.test.ts` 4 more cases, `trash.test.ts` a note from a deleted folder (failed before); protect, trash, file-ops, workspace, agent-host and the weight budget 1570 passed |
| SEC30 | High | A note the account put back under another name, because its name was taken meanwhile, came back unlocked and left its versions away (re-audit 7) | Fixed `0f2cf9fa` | The trash asked for the buried lock at the path it was deleted from and landed it there, never where the account put the note | The trash takes the path the account answers with; the lookup asks the old place and lands the locks and versions at the new one | `buried-history.test.ts` "under another name", `trash.test.ts` "puts back under another name" (failed before); protect, trash, workspace, sync-client, agent-host, file-ops and the weight budget 2009 passed |
| SEC31 | Medium | Undoing a copy or an import, and a file another device deleted, buried a lock with no trash id, so putting that item back from Recently deleted under another name came back unlocked (re-audit 7) | Fixed `0f2cf9fa` | `uncopy`, `unimport` and the sync disk's `remove` threw away the id `trash_item` answered | Each passes the trash id on to `fileGone` | `undoing.test.ts` copy and import, `app-world.test.ts` "tells which trash entry holds it" (failed before) |
| SEC32 | Medium | A note deleted from inside a locked folder came back unlocked once the folder was renamed meanwhile, and every buried lock of a renamed space stayed under its old root (re-audit 7) | Fixed `0f2cf9fa` | `gone` buried only locks held at or under the note, so a lock covering it from a folder above stayed behind and moved with the folder; `spaceMoved` rekeyed live locks, never buried ones | A deleted note buries the lock that covers it as its own; a renamed space carries its buried locks and versions to the new root | `buried-history.test.ts` "after the folder is renamed", "follow their space" (failed before) |
| SEC33 | Medium | An agent could put back a locked note from Recently deleted, or undo its delete, and read it in the moment before its lock was back (re-audit 7) | Fixed `0f2cf9fa` | The lock comes back after the file, asynchronously, so a parallel read saw an unlocked note | `recently_deleted restore` refuses an item a buried lock waits for (by trash id, by place, or by a folder above that went with it); `undo-file` and `redo-file` refuse to agents while any lock is buried | `deleted.test.ts` 3 cases, `commands.test.ts` 2 cases (failed before) |
| SEC34 | High | A locked note deleted on one device and put back from the account on another comes back unlocked there (re-audit 7) | Open, waiting on David | Burials live in each device's local storage; the account's lock record drops the lock when the note goes | Two ways, a product choice: keep the lock on the account record until the trash is emptied (a new note at that name is locked too), or keep burials in the account (a server change) | Traced, not reproduced across two devices |
| SEC35 | Medium | A lock could vanish from the account and every device: a push of the space's locks that failed after an older one landed, pushes the server took out of order, or a pull the account answered before a push landed all left the older map, which the next pull took as the truth, and agents could then read the note (re-audit 8) | Fixed `e44528f8` | Every change started its own PUT of the whole map; any PUT that landed marked the space sent whatever the map was now; a pull adopted what the account answered even if a push had gone out or landed meanwhile | Pushes of one space go one at a time, each reading the map as it runs; a push marks the space sent only when the map it sent is the one held now; a pull leaves a space alone when a push went out or landed while it waited | `pushes.test.ts` 3 cases (each failed before); protect, trash, workspace, sync-client, agent-host, file-ops, space-kept and the weight budget 2177 passed |
| SEC36 | Medium | Two locked notes deleted one after the other from the same name shared one burial, so when the account put both back the second came back unlocked and its versions went to the first (re-audit 8) | Fixed `e44528f8` | A burial was kept under its place name, which the second overwrote, and the account's restore can look up only by place | Each burial keeps its own place entry; a restore takes the newest at that place and drops only that burial's twin | `buried-history.test.ts` "of two locked notes deleted from one place" (failed before) |
| SEC37 | Medium | A lock set on one device vanished when another device, holding an older map, pushed any change of its own: the account kept only the second device's whole map, and the first device's next pull dropped the lock there too, opening the note to agents (re-audit 9) | Fixed `714680ae`, `5161f2c1` (the sync server's `protected.ts`, #2140's area) | Each device sends its whole map and the server put it in place of what it held | A push carries the map its device last had from the account; the server keeps what changed elsewhere since then and applies only this device's adds, changes and removals, and answers with the result, which the device takes as its own. A push without a base is taken whole as before | server `protected.test.ts` 2 cases, `pushes.test.ts` "carry what the account last held" (failed before); protected and MCP protected 15 passed, sync `tsc` clean |
| SEC38 | Medium | Putting back an unlocked note deleted from a place where a locked note had been deleted took that note's burial, so the locked note came back unlocked beside it (re-audit 9) | Fixed `5161f2c1` | A trash id with no burial fell back to the newest burial at the place, and taking it dropped the burial's own trash id | An unknown trash id falls back only to a burial that has no trash id of its own; the account's note or space id is kept on each burial and the account's restore asks by it | `buried-history.test.ts` "stay its own when an unlocked note ... is put back first" (failed before); `trash.test.ts` account cases 32 passed |
| SEC39 | Medium | A lock moved, copied or put back onto a path longer than 300 characters was dropped on the next start and by the account, and the note opened to agents (re-audit 9) | Fixed `714680ae`, `5161f2c1` | Only `set` refused long keys; the readers of the stored and the account's map dropped them silently | The stored map, the account's map and burials keep a key too long to hold as the folder above it that fits (the space when none does), so the note stays covered | `long-paths.test.ts` 2 cases, server "keeps a path too long to hold" (failed before) |
| SEC40 | High | A locked note deleted while signed in, which the account holds, came back unlocked when put back from the account's Recently deleted, and its versions stayed away (re-audit 10; SEC38 made it so) | Fixed `638ede7d` | The burial asked sync for the note's account id after sync had already forgotten the path, so it was kept under its place and a random id only; the account's restore asks by the note id, and SEC38's rule refuses a place burial that has an id of its own | An account delete takes the note's account id as its trash id before it deletes, so the burial is kept under the id the account's restore asks by | `trash.test.ts` account cases with sync forgetting the note once it is deleted (failed before); protect, trash, workspace, sync-client, agent-host, file-ops and the weight budget 2018 passed |

| SEC92 | Medium | A locked folder renamed during a burst the watcher answered with a full rescan lost its lock (re-audit 20) | Fixed `ac8026c5` | A rescan replaced what was known and said only "look again", so every rename in it was lost | Before the rescan is told, the old and new listings are compared by identity and the top-most moves are reported as renames | `known.rs` `a_space_read_again_names_what_moved_since_and_not_what_moved_with_it` |
| SEC93 | Medium | A listed program typed into a terminal tab (`run_terminal` with a tab, `type_terminal`) ran without asking where locked notes sat, past SEC80, SEC83 and SEC86 (re-audit 20) | Fixed `6cd58628` | The typed line was judged by the program list alone; the folder its shell is in is never known to Orden, so no lock check could run | A typed command line always asks; only a lone Ctrl+C goes unasked. `run_terminal` without a tab and `process` keep their free runs | `keys.test.ts` new cases (red-checked) |
| SEC94 | Medium | On Windows a listed program could reach another space with `..` written with backslashes, or `%VAR%` in Command Prompt (re-audit 20) | Fixed `6cd58628` | Words were split as a POSIX shell does, so `sub\..\..\Other` was checked as `sub....Other` | On Windows a line with a backslash, `^`, `,` or a `%NAME%` asks | `keys.test.ts` new case (red-checked) |
| SEC95 | Medium | git ran free from a linked worktree or below a stray `.git` folder, reading locked notes of the repository it really uses (re-audit 20) | Fixed `6cd58628` | The first `.git` entry found was taken as the repository top | A `.git` counts only when it holds `HEAD`, `objects` and `refs`; anything else there (a worktree's or submodule's `.git` file, a stray folder) means git is not free | `program-free.test.ts` new case (red-checked) |
| SEC96 | Medium | A listed program outside the known tables (`cat`, `rg`, `head`) read `../Private/Diary.md` or an absolute path without asking (re-audit 20) | Fixed `6cd58628` | The path rule ran only for git and the package managers | Every listed program's words, and the values of its options, may not name a path leaving the folder | `program-free.test.ts`, `keys.test.ts` new cases (red-checked) |
| SEC97 | Medium | A locked note moved out of a folder that was renamed in the same rescan lost its lock (SEC92 incomplete) (re-audit 21) | Fixed `b308a65a` | Each move named its old place from the old listing, though the folder's move, told first, had already carried its key | A move's old place is taken through the moves already told | `known.rs` `a_space_read_again_names_what_moved_since_and_not_what_moved_with_it` (extended) |
| SEC98 | Medium | A locked note renamed over another inside a folder renamed in the same batch lost its lock (SEC90 incomplete) (re-audit 21) | Fixed `b308a65a` | The lock was copied before any change of the batch ran, so before the folder's rename had moved it | The copy runs in the removal's own turn, in both app watchers | `outside-moves-lock.test.ts` new case (red-checked) |
| SEC99 | Medium | A listed program reached a locked note or another space through a path behind `@` or `=`, or a package scheme (`curl -d @../Private/Diary.md`, `dd if=/abs`, `npm install x@file:../pkg`, `git+file://`) (SEC84 and SEC96 incomplete) (re-audit 21) | Fixed `042301b7` | Only a word's start was tested, and `..` only as a whole segment between `/`, `\` or `=` | A path start after `@` or `=`, a `file:`, `link:` or `portal:` anywhere, and `..` between any of `/ \ = @ :` reach out | `keys.test.ts` new case (red-checked) |
| SEC100 | Medium | On Windows with Command Prompt, single quotes hid a path from the judge (re-audit 21) | Fixed `042301b7` | The judge read `'` as quoting, which Command Prompt and the C runtime do not | On Windows a line with `'` asks | `keys.test.ts`, `shell-text.test.ts` new cases (red-checked) |

| SEC137 | Medium | A member making a folder of the same name as a link the reader keeps in a space (`Refs -> /elsewhere`) could then read, rename and delete through it what lies behind the link, outside the space, as every other path command only judged how the path reads (complete re-audit, SEC135 sibling) | Fixed `c06d4e97` | SEC135 fenced four writes one by one; reads, renames and the rest still took the path as written | Every path a command judges inside a space is fenced by where its nearest existing folder really is, so no command goes through a link out of its space (a note that is itself a link still saves) | `paths.rs` test (red-checked), full Rust lib tests |
