| SEC11 | Low | hono 4.13.5 (RegExpRouter wildcard middleware, `mount`, `serveStatic`, `csrf` advisories) and DOMPurify 3.4.14 under mermaid (IN_PLACE and SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES advisories) | Fixed `f6da77a5` | Old versions; no affected route shape (`/x*`, mount, serveStatic) and no DOMPurify mode mermaid uses was found, so not reachable as far as traced | hono ^4.13.16, `mermaid>dompurify` ^3.4.17 | sync suite 3063 passed |

### Checked and left (Low or intended)

- Re-audit 19, Low: input a started program gets later (`write`, `stdin`) is not judged line by line as the terminal's typed lines are (no listed program the judge restricts runs commands from its input); the lock check of a run's folder takes the path as given, so a link inside a space to a locked folder passes (the known symlink Low); a `process` run has no time limit; a free `npm install <pkg>` or `pnpm add <pkg>` from the registry runs that package's install scripts, as the terminal judge has always allowed (an owner decision whether a listed npm should cover it). Also from the lock review: a signed-in space the engine has not paired, or whose first scan is still running, has no watcher carrying locks for outside renames (the SEC81 class there); a name with a backslash or control character cannot be locked and `set` says nothing; v1 sync's folded delete tells no trash id.
- Re-audit 20, Low: the lock store's 400-lock cap could drop keys silently past it; renames made by `git checkout` or `git pull` arrive as removed and created (no shared identity), so a lock does not follow those; a move into another space that the watcher splits across two batches, or that lands during a rescan of one side, stays a removal and a new note; a move into a hidden folder of another space is a removal; in Confirm mode with the Terminal ask switched off by hand-editing a grant, a non-free `run_terminal` asks nothing while `process` asks as writing (no setting reaches it). Unconfirmed: with Command Prompt as the shell, a `git.bat` brought in by a free `git clone` would run for a later `git` typed there (typed lines now always ask, so only through the reader's own typing); a free `git checkout -- .` or `reset --hard` overwrites notes without the deleting ask that `restore_version` uses.
- Re-audit 21, Low: a move into a hidden folder in a batch that is cut or answered by a rescan is still a removal (rescans never list hidden names); a folder renamed over an empty folder is announced only as removed (the watcher looks past folders that stay); git history still holds a locked note the reader deleted for good (no lock is kept for it once Recently deleted is emptied); the explorer verb was not reviewed this round (a reviewer was interrupted) and goes to the next.
- Re-audit 22, Low: git history of a locked note moved into another space stays readable from the old space's repository; `pnpm --filter {../x}` passes the `process` judge but reaches no further than `--filter <name>`; a version range such as `lodash@~4.17.0` or a commit message holding `link:` now asks (stricter, not unsafe). From the untrusted-content review: a clipped web page or an imported note becomes the reader's own note and is read unmarked from then on (its `source:` front matter is not used for trust); marking those is an owner decision. The CSP, IPC capabilities, link schemes and rendering of others' notes were checked and found right.
- Re-audit 23, Low: a dirty note a collaborator deletes is kept as a draft with no source, so it is read later as the reader's own; chat preview titles set by other clients show as sent; `browser_navigate` answers name the URL unmarked; refusals from the explorer and from locked waits name the file asked about. A reviewer looking at what a planted dot folder could reach was stopped once SEC109 made such folders never land.
- Re-audit 24, Low: a reaction may be any 64 characters without spaces and is shown unmarked in a message's head line (it cannot break the line); a restore from Recently deleted echoes the name already listed marked; `move_file` rewrites links inside locked notes with the new name (nothing is read back); tree commands run through `run_command` open sheets the reader drives without checking the front note's lock; a deleted shared space's name is listed unmarked; an explorer copy redone from the redo list is judged as a read, not a change.
- Re-audit 25, Low: a lock taken on a note after it was gathered into a thread does not reach back into that thread's earlier turns, which are sent again on every turn, to another model after a switch, and into compaction summaries (later sends, queued sends, drafts and quotes are judged again); desktop `list_spaces` names spaces shared with the reader unmarked (one line each); a folder chip lists the names of locked notes; `/catchup` names locked chats when it lists them (their messages are not sent). Not verified: dictation's prompt from the text before the cursor, the Even glasses answers, local CLI providers.
- Re-audit 26, Low: `/batch` puts the names of locked notes that match a tag into helper prompts; priority lessons keep a reassigned chat's name; Codex as a local provider keeps its own `view_image` tool (not verified). From the prompt review: a web tab chip's title can hold a line separator inside its quoted source; an AI block or a rewrite in a collaborator's note sends it unmarked to a model with no tools, whose answer lands where the collaborator can already write.
- Re-audit 27, Low: a local CLI provider's prompt wraps turns in its own tags (`<user>`, `<conversation>`) that nothing defuses, and the goal check cuts tool text so a mark can lose its opening (no tools there, so at most a forged goal verdict); a prompt that copies a mark word for word, or a collaborator's AGENTS.md read into the system prompt, does not count a thread as having read others' words; defusing changes `<summary>` in the reader's own note as the model sees it; a summary of a thread that read others' words, including the reader's own limits in it, is now marked as a whole; a provider's own compaction block is replayed unmarked. a server lock now also covers a folder that differs only in case on a case-sensitive disk (stricter); a collaborator moving the reader's locked note out of its folder leaves the server's lock behind until a device of the reader's pulls (the collaborator could read it already).
- Re-audit 28, Low: the scanner leaves out clutter (`~$*`, `Thumbs.db`, `desktop.ini`) that the receiver still places, though it takes over a local one only with the same bytes; `.Inbox.md` is carried by name at any depth, so a member can make one in a subfolder; a server answer refusing a binary file by role deletes the reader's changed copy without a snapshot; Linux is taken never to fold case, even on exFAT or casefold mounts; a link inside a space that leads into another space is followed.
- Re-audit 29, Low: a note that is itself a link out of its space briefly could not be saved after SEC135 (fixed `25284486`: the save replaces the link); a folder kept by SEC134 comes back for every member, empty, at the next full pass, and a file it failed to remove is sent again as new; renaming an already shared note to a dot name still sends the rename and the dot folder (its words were shared already); Siri and Shortcuts add to-dos to the front space's `.Inbox.md`, which its members see when the space is shared; an `.application` note carries the program's local path.
- Complete re-audit (30), Low: the Rust marks kept line, paragraph and format characters in a mark's source and in one-line names, which the app's own marks drop (fixed `73ae151c`); the free-run judge checks locks by the folder as written, so a link the reader made inside a space to a locked folder of the same space is an unlocked alias for a listed program (as the known re-audit 4 link Low); an interpreter the reader puts on the allow list runs any code it is given (the reader's own list). From the full suites: three checks failed on this branch's own changes, a terminal test still expecting a listed typed line to go unasked (SEC93 asks for every typed line), the lock map compared as raw stored text, and the startup weight 100 bytes over budget; all three fixed in `4fddd6be`. One sync room test failed once under load and passes alone (untouched here).
- Regression review (30), Low: a note that is itself a file link out of its space is still read through the link, as the tree lists it (the reader made it, and agents cannot make links in a space); a lock in a shared space now covers every case spelling on Linux too (fails toward refusing); a trusted attached note holding `<summary>` reaches the model as `&lt;summary>`; chat sources drop zero-width joiners, so an emoji name is split in a mark's source (cosmetic).
- Re-audit 31, Low: on Linux the file watcher follows folder links, so the name of a file behind a link out of a space can be sent to members as an entry (its bytes are refused); copying a folder in the app turns a file link inside it into a real file, which then syncs (the reader's own copy); nothing opens files with no-follow, so a link swapped between the check and the write is not caught (only the process verb, which already has a shell, can make links).
- Re-audit 32 (regressions of the link fences), no Medium or higher. Low: the app's folder existence check falls back to reading the tree, which now refuses a folder link out of its space, so a name held only by such a link reads as free and a note written to exactly that name replaces the link with a file (what lay behind the link is untouched); saving a picture onto a dangling file link writes where the link points (as the known no-follow Low). Behavior that changed on purpose: notes, pictures and search hits inside a folder linked in from outside a space can no longer be read, moved or found (the tree already showed such folders as unreachable), and pasting a picture fails with a message when the attachments folder is such a link.
- Re-audit 18, Low: a delete drops its lock keys a few milliseconds before the old versions are moved away; if the window that started a put-back or delete closes before it finishes, no other window finishes it; two windows changing locks within milliseconds can still overwrite each other, as localStorage between processes is no fence; a lock set inside a conflict-copy folder does not cover a copy of a note inside it; the agent-reach review of this round was stopped twice by a safety classifier, so only the process and git judge points were covered by it.
- Re-audit 17, Low: the helper site comparison normalizes keys more simply than Rust (IDN, ports, repeated `*.`, ties), which only hand-made grants reach, since Settings stores plain lowercase domains; `workspace_tabs open` with a URL opens a page in the reader's store with only `workspace` (deny and agent-store rules still apply); `note_editor select` plus `selection` reads text without `notes.read` (needs `workspace.focus` and `editor`, as designed); a helper started with no thread takes its mode from the grant; a Ctrl+V into the agent's own page might paste the reader's clipboard (engine dependent, not reproduced); a folder named `X.pages` carries the lock of a sibling `X.pages.pdf`, and a page note renamed off `.pages` leaves its paper covered only by its own key; a large account folder delete whose listing is skipped buries by place names only.
- Re-audit 16, Low: `copiesKept` sees only the open space's tree, so a foreign copy beside a locked note moved in a space that is not open keeps only its name-mapped lock; a page note's deleted paper leaves its lock key behind (more locked, not less); `agent_result` answers any helper by its random id; a helper with no thread loads profiles from the active space; the process ask shows bidi or zero-width characters raw and the program as typed; helper limits (tabs, calls) are not compared.
- Re-audit 15, Low: `write_setting` can unbind the Stop-agents shortcut or change the address-bar search engine (needs `settings`, asked); `run_command` asks are keyed by the command and show its label, so its target (the front note) can change before it runs (share, publish and send open sheets the reader drives); `ask_reader` notices carry the agent's own name; `workspace_tabs list` still names a shut copy's title; an "Always" for a folder covers the folders inside it; a path-less copy whose note is deleted keeps pointing at the old path, which is buried; `reachesNothingShut` and `frontShut` look at the front tab's path, not the note behind a copy; sharing a locked note asks for no unlock (a product choice, as export); short windows where a rename, copy, merge or keep both writes the new file a moment before its lock; iCloud "Name 2" and Google Drive "Name (1)" conflict copies are not known as foreign copies; `paper.pdf.highlights.json` beside a locked PDF is not covered (as the attachments Low); the publish sheet does not mark locked notes it will leave out (new UI, not built).
- Re-audit 14, Low: a member's agents and the server MCP do not see the owner's locks in a shared space (each reader's locks are their own gate; intended, written here); a note moved from a folder shut to agents into a locked folder opened to agents takes that folder's weaker grant until it relocks; a lock pull carrying another device's rename can land before the move does (as the re-audit 9 delete-ordering Low); about 400 long lock paths fill the account's 64 KB cap and stop lock pushes; choosing "theirs" on a held locked file buries its lock until the account's copy revives it; `explorer_tabs` lists folders inside any space, with names, sizes and times, without a space or lock check (needs `files`, Experimental); `system_clipboard read` returns what the reader copied from a locked note (the `clipboard` scope); `workspace_tabs list kind:online` names a locked session's path (`read_terminal` refuses it); `bookmarks list` names locked paths; a copy's lock follows the note's path at the time it was copied, not a later rename.
- Re-audit 13, Low: a note sync brings back sits unlocked on disk for the moment between the write and its revival (as the known undone-merge window); burial aliases for deletes that bury nothing stay in memory; a revived note's burial now stays until its deleted copy is put back or purged, so agents' undo stays refused meanwhile, and a second revival of that id locks it again even if the reader took the lock off; `read_note {tab: 'scratchpad'}` reads the one scratchpad of every space (it lies in none); plan mode narrows only the tools offered to the built-in agent (not traced further); `browser_upload` now refuses Orden's own folders (fixed with SEC53, traced, no Rust test since it needs the app handle).
- Verb audit, Low: `get_context` hands the selection of the front tab without checking that its note is in the agent's spaces (only its lock); `set_property` of an icon or cover on a path-less draft sets it on the space root; `add_row` can make a hidden `.x.md` note, and on Windows a reserved name like `CON`; reading verbs (`read_note`, `read_pdf`, `read_canvas`, `list_notes`) reach hidden files inside a space (locks still apply; the trash and `.orden` are refused by Rust); `create_note` of a canvas, pages or web note can land in a locked folder (integrity only); `query_base` with `yaml` skips the path guard (DoS only); `write_setting general.relockAfter` can keep locks open until Orden quits (needs the `settings` scope); `workspace_tabs new` of an online or terminal tab needs only `workspace` (typing needs `terminal`).
- Re-audit 12, Low: bases kept before SEC42 are dropped, so a device's pushes go without a base until its first lock pull (as the known renamed-space Low); the server matches lock keys exactly while the desktop folds case and Unicode form, so a case-only outside rename on a folding disk is open to the server MCP (close to the outside-rename Low); a pull answered before an unlock push lands folds the old lock back in (integrity only, the unlock is undone).
- Re-audit 11 and complete re-audit, Low: undoing an account delete takes the burial while sync puts the account's note back, so if that revive fails (its record pruned, the engine stopped) the account's copy comes back unlocked (traced only); the server's mend moves a child note out of a duplicate folder without its own lock until a desktop pushes it (sync thread's area); a space at the 400 lock cap drops the newest lock from the merged answer; agent space reach and rows compare space names, so two spaces of one name are both reached; a space writer can dress the owner's site with a stylesheet or script hash of their own, or drop `publish.js` from its files; a refused read-role op id is still kept as done; link previews do not refuse `::7f00:1`.
- Re-audit 10, Low: a lock on a note or folder named `__proto__` is dropped by the stored map, the account and burials (needs that exact name); a lock named like an inherited key (`constructor`, `toString`) cannot be taken away through a merged push (it stays, integrity only); the base is kept by folder, not by account, so a first push after signing into another account may carry a stale base until the first pull (not reproduced).
- Re-audit 9, Low: each burial now takes two to three entries of the 1000 kept, so several hundred deletes under a locked folder could push out an older note's burial still in the trash; a lock pull landing before another device's delete reaches this disk leaves the note here unlocked until it does (close to SEC34); an agent's restore of an account item finds its space by name, the trash by remote id (no leak window found); `run_command archive` sets a flag on a locked note in front (integrity only); a space renamed on this device sends its next lock push without a base, taken whole as before; more than 400 locks in one space are cut at 400 by the account.
- Re-audit 8, Low: a dirty open locked note deleted outside Orden becomes a path-less draft tab that `read_note {tab}` reads (needs an unsaved edit in the autosave window plus an outside delete); `query_base` looks rows up by space name, so two spaces of one name (a kept space and a new one) can mix rows (not reproduced); unlocks and "Let agents read" are keyed by path and outlive a delete, so a later lock at that path opens at once (needs the reader to lock a new note at that name); notes made from a template in an unlocked locked folder land unlocked (the reader's pick); `capture_to_note` and `attach_agent_log` write into locked notes (integrity only, nothing is read back); when both are put back, two burials at one place may hand each other's way or versions (both stay locked).
- Re-audit 7, Low: agents may not undo or redo file changes while any lock is buried, and a burial whose item was purged stays until it is put back (the reader can still undo); a failed rehome of versions is swallowed (they stay away, not leaked); `put` of a space's locks has no size cap; the oldest of more than 1000 burials is dropped; the account putting back a space that was deleted on this device finds its burial only by place; an NFD spelling of a folder name may not slice its versions' path.
- Re-audit 6, Low: two windows following one delete would point the versions at an empty folder (lost, not leaked); a history whose origin was spelled in another case on a folding disk stays where it is when its note is buried (not verified, agents cannot write one); versions taken in the same millisecond as one already at a move's destination stay behind.
- Re-audit 5, Low: undoing the delete of an account-held folder whose note's name was taken meanwhile writes the note beside and gives the lock to the newcomer (agents can no longer delete a locked folder, SEC27); an undone merge writes the note a moment before its lock is back; a move synced from another device as a remove and a write is unlocked until that device's lock record arrives (not verified); names that Windows or APFS fold by their own tables (σ and ς, ſ and s) may spell a locked folder another way (not verified).
- Re-audit 4, Low: attachments beside a note-level lock are not covered by it (a PDF attached to a locked note reads with `read_pdf`); a symlink inside a space gives a locked folder an unlocked alias (needs an outside-made link); a command given a path that opens no dialog keeps the path armed for 15 s for the reader's next save dialog.
- Final re-audit 3, Low: members' open room sockets stay until they disconnect after the owner trashes the space (the room re-check reads `space_members`, which a restore needs; reconnecting is refused); an unknown host still pointed at the service gets the app and API (needs a reader to sign in on a foreign domain); any OAuth client may call itself "Orden ..." (the consent page names the redirect host); a reader's own note HTML keeps `on*` attributes, which the CSP refuses to run; AMO's signature check is a substring test behind a SHA-256 match against the HTTPS listing; outside renames move notes out from under path-keyed locks.
- Final re-audit, Low: `failed.ts` logs the raw path of a 500, which can hold a join link token or Pass link id; MCP `list_notes` names protected notes (never their words; read, search and backlinks hold them); an SVG blob whose bytes do not match its hash is taken under a hash nobody holds yet (intended, `blobs.test.ts`); chat messages load remote pictures and diagram pictures as plain markdown in chats always has (the sender learns the reader's IP and time); the browser build's "Show it here" frames do not refuse the app's own origin (nothing on it serves a reader's HTML unpublished); link previews do not refuse NAT64 `64:ff9b::/96` or `2002::/16`.
- Token-connected agents (MCP clients) run in `Mode::Autonomous` whatever their grant says (`agents/mod.rs` `connected`). Intended and tested (`a_client_connected_over_mcp_is_asked_nothing`): such a client already runs code on the same machine.
- Exporting a space in the browser copies protected notes as files when the reader runs it. Protection is a gate in the app, not encryption; the files are plain on a desktop disk too. Low; asking for the unlock first is a product choice.
- Server, Low: OAuth `redirect_uri` optional at the token endpoint (PKCE S256 is required and the code is bound and single-use); a reused refresh token revokes its grant (RFC 9700 reuse detection); IDNA and NFC spellings of one address make two accounts (the code always goes to the folded address); a guest token is claimed before the second factor (needs the email code); turning on the second factor needs no fresh email code; site password guessing can lock readers out for an hour; `?channel=` switches a visitor's channel without a check; the fallback mailer prints codes when `EMAIL` is unbound (production binds it).
- Pass, Low: vouches and past owners never expire; "hide passwords from viewers" hides them only in the UI; a writer could race to bind a new terminal note to their own machine; a machine's link secret is never rotated and every typist can read it with sudo; `people/key` says whether an address has Pass.
- Pass, design: a person never seen before is trusted on first use, so an account could list a member who never joined during a rotation, as it could substitute a key on a first share. Safety codes are the check.
- Desktop and server, Low (re-audit): `automation.json` keeps one secret across runs and is left behind when Orden quits, so another local user listening on the old port while Orden is closed can catch it (single-user machines are not affected); feedback and crash intake stop for everybody at their daily ceilings, and a report's status page asks GitHub on every view; a script on a site subdomain can set `orden-channel` or oversized cookies for `orden.so`; the "Run this program?" sheet does not show the working folder a `.application` file names; a protected site's own CSS and JS are served by hash without its ticket; tokens made before tokens named a session survive "end every other session" (kept on purpose, `mcp.test.ts`); feedback issues carry the account's address into `lxorb/orden-feedback`, which must stay private (not checked from here).
- Client, Low: Tauri's startup globals are visible to pages in web tabs (commands are refused by origin); untrusted notes still offer click-to-load iframe cards; `'unsafe-eval'` stays in the main CSP for automation `eval`; account tokens live in `localStorage`.
- Dependencies, Low or not shipped: `zip` 4.6.1 extract advisories (Orden never calls `extract`; extensions use their own unzip, dictation reads by name); `libsqlite3-sys` 0.30 needs a crafted database and WAL pair opened locally; KaTeX 0.16 under mermaid (prototype pollution must already exist); devalue (Svelte SSR only); undici under jsdom, vite, vitest, eslint's brace-expansion and minimatch, wrangler's sharp, the Even CLI's zod are dev tools only; unmaintained `unic-*`, `zbus_names`, `zvariant`, `proc-macro-error`, `fxhash`.

## Desktop backend

Burn-down of 2026-11-15 over `apps/desktop/src-tauri` (the Tauri commands, files, explorer,
agents, downloads, search, terminal, sync store and the module crates), from `unstable`.
Audit: four read-only passes over the whole crate, each finding traced to an input before it
was kept, then one review of the fixes and a re-audit of what the first passes read only in
part. Each fix has a test that failed before it, unless the row says otherwise. Checks: `cargo
test`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` on the CEF manifest
(`scripts/linux-cef.sh`), Linux x86_64, as root.

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DB1 | High | An agent given "always" for one folder could copy or move a file from it to any folder without being asked, e.g. into `~/.config/autostart` | Fixed `79d784c5`, `9a7cb206` | `question` checked only the source folder against the always list | Copy and move ask unless both the source and the destination folder are always allowed; the question names the folder not yet allowed, so "always" can cover it | `a_folder_always_allowed_does_not_let_a_copy_or_move_write_elsewhere` |
| DB2 | High | `browser upload` could hand a file from Orden's own folders (`automation.json` with the endpoint secret, `agents.json`, cookies) to a page | Fixed `45daabfb`, `a27304fb`, `6033043e` | `files_of` never applied the own-folder fence the `files` and `system` verbs apply | An agent's upload is refused for any path behind Orden's own folders, by spelling and by real path, except inside the spaces folder (which on a fresh install lives in the app's data folder; the first fix refused it, the review caught it); a link in a space to an own file is still refused. The agent `system` verb's open and reveal had the same spaces problem and use the same rule | `a_file_of_ordens_own_folder_is_never_handed_to_a_page` (the helper; the missing call was found by reading) |
| DB3 | Medium | `cargo test` did not build on `unstable` | Fixed `33cfb77c` | `page_scale` test `include_str!` named `src/lib/modes.svelte.ts`, which moved into the modes module | Reads `src/lib/modes/src/modes.svelte.ts` | the crate's tests build and run |
| DB4 | Medium | On macOS the explorer's untrash renamed any path the page named as a ticket's `back`, so a forged ticket could move Orden's own files out | Fixed `f1e49359` | Tickets were trusted as data; only `ticket.path` was judged | Only tickets the explorer issued are taken back, once; a failed put-back keeps its ticket | `a_ticket_the_trash_never_gave_brings_nothing_back`, `a_ticket_the_trash_gave_is_taken_back_once`. The macOS rename itself was not run here |
| DB5 | Medium | Copying or moving a file into its own folder reached through a symlink took it for a clash; Replace trashed the source | Fixed `d695fb74`, `3c978a91`, `13774cbd` | `same()` compared spellings only | `same()` also holds for the same file system entry or the same real path when the destination is not itself a link (a link carried next to what it points at; moving a folder onto a link to it stays a clash) | `a_file_carried_into_its_own_folder_by_another_way_is_never_replaced_by_itself` |
| DB6 | Medium | A download, a saved picture or a new space with a long name in a non-Latin script, or a long extension, failed to save (name over 255 bytes); a long picture name also lost its extension | Fixed `c4e71748`, `6c8287aa`, `9359a748` | The limits (180 for downloads, 120 for pictures) counted chars, and the extension was outside them | Both count bytes, cut on a char boundary, and keep a short extension | `a_long_name_in_any_script_fits_a_file_system_name`, `assets` long-name test, `a_name_of_wide_letters_still_fits_a_file_system_name` |
| DB7 | Medium | Pattern search missed matches in notes of a few KB, and `not(pattern)` then matched the note | Fixed `14c48c79`, `575c4017` | One fixed 250k-step budget per note, and running out read as "no match" | The budget grows with the note (400 steps a byte, 250k to 10M); a not-term is undecided only when a pattern inside it ran out. Cost: a hopeless pattern over many large notes now spends up to 40 times the old steps per note | `a_pattern_finds_its_match_in_a_note_of_a_few_pages`, `a_pattern_that_runs_out_never_counts_as_missing` (both failed before); the catastrophic-pattern timing test still passes |
| DB8 | Low | `delete_folder` and `remove_empty_folder` accepted the spaces folder itself and `.orden` inside it | Fixed `09d85127`, `eee69d1b` | `in_spaces` takes the root too | `below_spaces` refuses the root and `.orden` itself; what is inside `.orden` (kept spaces, their working copies) stays deletable. The first fix refused all of `.orden` and broke deleting a kept space; the review caught it | `neither_the_spaces_folder_nor_its_own_folder_is_taken_away_whole` |
| DB9 | Low | A named pipe called `*.md` in a space hung the link index, a click on it and a save to it | Fixed `5542efd9` | Blocking `open` before the file-type check | Reads open non-blocking and refuse what is not a file; a save writes in place only over a plain file | `a_pipe_named_like_a_note_is_neither_waited_on_nor_written_into` (timed out before) |
| DB10 | Medium | A `blob:` frame, tab or history step of a denied site passed the agent's site rules (a denied bank's PDF preview) | Fixed `047959e0`, `33fb5713` | `frame_allowed`, `site_allowed` and `from_allowed` read a host only from http(s) URLs | `made_by` judges a blob URL by the origin inside it, everywhere | `a_denied_site_in_a_frame_is_denied` and the site rule test (new rows failed before) |
| DB11 | Low | The agent http verb's pinned address was missed for a host with a trailing dot, so reqwest looked the name up again | Fixed `f862748e` | The override key had the dot trimmed; reqwest matches the URI host exactly | The key is the URL's own host | `the_address_looked_up_is_pinned_under_the_name_the_request_asks_for` |
| DB12 | Low | Find in page changed a term containing `__BACK__` or `__FRESH__` | Fixed `ff57d2b9` | The term was put in before the flags were replaced | Flags first, term last | `a_word_that_reads_like_a_flag_is_looked_for_as_written` |
| DB13 | Low | A chat thread with id `index` overwrote the thread list | Fixed `dfab3651` | Thread files and the list shared one namespace | Thread ids refuse `index` in any case | `a_thread_never_takes_the_place_of_the_list` |
| DB14 | Low | `reminders.json` and the hosted apps' allowed list were written in place, so a crash mid-write lost them | Fixed `2e9cdb3d`, `3d810abe` | `fs::write` | `write_atomically` | by reading; no crash test |
| DB15 | Low | Changing the window frame also framed the undecorated quick add panel | Fixed `1fd164f1` | `set_frame` walked every window | Only document windows, the ones built with `own_frame` | by reading; not run in a window |
| DB16 | Low | `cargo clippy -D warnings` failed on current stable (`float_cmp` in placement tests) | Fixed `0c0f5e83` | Exact float compare in two asserts | Compare within `f64::EPSILON` | clippy clean |
| DB17 | Medium | An agent's snapshot of one element inside a same-process iframe always answered "that element is gone" | Fixed `a4af03b0` | `holds` and `render` looked only in the first part with the element's prefix, and same-process frames share the empty prefix | Every part with the prefix is searched | `an_element_of_a_frame_in_the_page_s_own_process_is_held_and_written` |
| DB18 | Medium | In a freshly opened web tab Back and Forward navigated by address: after a redirect Back stayed lit and did nothing, and in-page history was skipped | Fixed `de400444`, `a5726979` | `Trail::default()` set `engine: false`, unlike `Trail::restored` for a short trail; and the address asked for stayed a step of its own when the load landed elsewhere | A fresh trail steps through the engine's history, and the address asked for is replaced by where its load landed | `a_tab_opened_fresh_steps_through_the_engine_s_own_history`, `an_address_that_redirects_is_one_step_where_it_landed` |
| DB19 | Medium | Search by property missed block lists (`authors:` then `- Herbert`), flow lists (`tags: [a, b]`), quoted values and values with a comment | Fixed `d7116315` | `front_matter` kept the raw text after the colon of top-level lines only | Every key reads its members through `front_matter::list`, which now drops a trailing comment the way the web twin's `uncommented` does; a property matches when any member does. Reported by the CLI/MCP thread; the web twin (`packages/scan` matcher and `tagsIn`) is fixed on that thread's branch, and until it lands, unsaved-note search and replace-all disagree with saved-note search on these notes | `front_matter_answers_for_every_member_of_a_list_and_a_value_however_written` |
| DB20 | Low | A comment after front matter tags (`- project # active`, `tags: [a] # old`) was read as a tag | Fixed `27bbade5` | `read_front` and `read_items` read the single-line rest and list items without `without_comment` | Both drop the comment first | `a_comment_after_the_tags_is_no_tag` |
| DB21 | Medium | Renaming a chain of three or more notes outside Orden in one watch batch (`mv c d; mv b c; mv a b`) reported the first as removed, so sync deleted it on the account and other devices, and the note at its new name went unwatched | Fixed `456d0609` | `settle` ran the arrival pass a fixed two times, and each pass resolves one link of the chain | The arrival passes repeat until one finds nothing new, at most once per touched path | `a_chain_of_renames_in_one_batch_is_every_rename_and_nothing_removed` (failed before: "removed a.md") |
| DB22 | Medium | Opening Recently deleted could erase a different note from the device trash: one the reader deleted at the same place after the account's copy went (a note made again and deleted before it synced, or one differing only in case on a case-sensitive disk) was taken for the account's copy and purged (F17, from the frontend audit) | Fixed `874cd97a` | A device `TrashEntry` did not say whether sync or the reader put it there, so `doublesOf` could only match on place and deletion time | `trash_item` takes `arrived`, the entry records it (`arrived` in the manifest, absent on older entries); the mirror and the sync client pass it when they apply a deletion from elsewhere, the web backend's twin stores it too, and `doublesOf` only takes entries sync made | `doubles.test.ts` reader's own entry at the same place (failed before), Rust tests for the recorded flag and an old manifest, web backend tests for both stores (failed before); vitest for trash, sync-client, sync-mirror, web-backend, svelte-check, eslint, full `cargo test`, clippy, fmt |

### Desktop backend status

| Severity | Found | Fixed | Open |
| --- | --: | --: | --: |
| Critical | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| High | 2 | 2 | 0 |
| Medium | 12 | 11 | 1, not reproduced |
| Low | about 40 | 9 | the rest accepted, listed below |

The open Medium is the Linux file drop below, which needs a real drag on a Linux build to
confirm. The last pass (a review of the second-round fixes and a re-audit of the areas the
first passes read in part) found no Critical or High; its Mediums are fixed above. Full
`cargo test` on the CEF manifest at the last fix: 1921 passed, 6 ignored, the one root-only permission test
passing as an ordinary user. Clippy and fmt clean. `pnpm test`: three failures outside this
crate, all of the container (no `ssh-keygen`, the machine service's fork-bomb limit, and
`tab-fill.effect` under load, which passes alone). This is what the audit checked, not a claim
that the backend is free of bugs.

### Desktop backend, open and accepted

- **Medium, not reproduced: file drops on Linux.** Dropping files from the system on a terminal or a PDF on the window does nothing on Linux. The main window keeps its drop handler off there (`launch.rs`), and nothing fills `DROPPED` since the WebView2 and WKWebView hooks left in #2097, so `dropped_paths` always answers nothing and the page's `droppedPaths` gives up after a second. macOS and Windows go through Tauri's own drop events. It needs a real drag on a Linux CEF build to confirm and a hook in the vendored CEF runtime to fix, which is the engine work #2097 is doing; left for that.
- **Low, accepted:** regex `.` takes `\r`, unlike the page's `RegExp` (an existing test asserts it); on Windows the cleanup of an older build's `.md\ShellNew` key removes any such key in HKCU, not only its own (not compiled or run here); on macOS `set_frame` hands the keyboard to the last document window it walks; the trash tickets the explorer hands out are kept for the session; clearing browsing data for a time range empties the whole cache; the Claude Code and Gemini capability cache is kept until restart; toast presses fall back to the main window after 64 notices; `orden-mcp.json` is rewritten on each agent session; CPU samples per terminal are never forgotten; hosted apps get no grace at quit; the stall watchdog can name the wrong command when calls overlap; `time_sense` hands a caller during a probe empty power data; "Move to Applications" launches an older copy that is already there; `outside.rs` merges into "name 500" when every name is taken; cookie verbs work per site, not per host; partitioned cookies survive an agent's clear; cookie values in an agent's storage answer carry no untrusted mark; the `system` verb refuses spaces paths on a fresh install with a misleading reason; a case-only rename on a case-insensitive disk is reported as a new file (not traced to the end); a large H.264 keyframe can be flushed in two parts after a 2 ms pause in the hosted app stream; the Windows stack walker can leave a thread suspended after a timeout (Windows, not run); a page that replaces its address from script after loading (`location.replace`) leaves the tab's trail one step longer than the engine's history; on a fresh install an agent's upload reaches the spaces trash, as it already did where the spaces live elsewhere; `delete_folder` accepts any folder inside `.orden` but `.orden` itself; per-site approvals and script rights on a `blob:` page are not matched to their site (the strict direction).
- **Not run on platform:** the macOS rename in DB4, the macOS keyboard part of DB15 and every Windows-only path. The checks ran on Linux x86_64 only.

## AI chat and providers

Burn-down of 2026-11-15 on `claude/project-thread-wpckqq`, from `unstable` at `fb58ff0a`. Scope: the AI chat panel and
store (`ai-chat`, `ai-sidebar`), AI providers and the key vault (`ai-providers`), agent tools (`agent-host`,
`agent-verbs`, `agent-protocol`, `agent-docs`, `agents`, `automation`) and protected notes (`protect`). Audits by area,
each finding traced to a concrete input; each fix reproduced first by a failing test (run against the old code), then
the area's tests, eslint and prettier on Node 24. Ids are `AI-n` so they do not collide with other threads' rows.

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AI-1 | High | An agent could move a note out of a protected folder, or trash a protected note and restore it, and then read it; the command line could also write over, append to, move or delete a protected note | Fixed `8012d69a` | `move_file`/`trash_file` checked only `writableAt`; a lock follows a move only for keys inside the moved item and `gone` drops the key; `files.write/move/delete` and `append` in automation never asked protection | `protection.agentsMayAll` (the path and every lock inside it); the two tree verbs and the four automation acts refuse what it refuses | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "is not moved out of its lock or trashed by an agent…", `acts.test.ts` "is not written over, moved out of its lock or deleted from the command line" (both failed before) |
| AI-2 | High | `run_command` with a dialog `path` exported the front note or the whole space to a file the agent could read, past "Let agents read" | Fixed `b2c24981` | With a path, export-* needed no scope and nothing asked protection; the exporters check only the reader's lock | `exportsOnlyWhatAgentsMay`: the front note must be readable by agents, `export-space` needs every lock in the space open to agents | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "is not exported by an agent to a path it can read…" (failed before) |
| AI-3 | High | `capture_to_note {folder: '../Secret'}` wrote into a space the agent does not reach, or a read-only or hidden folder | Fixed `4377ad6f` | `folder` went raw into `freshPath`; `within`'s prefix fast path then judged `/s/Work/../Secret` as inside Work | `folder` goes through `judgedForWriting` like every other agent path | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "lands in no folder outside the space, and in no hidden one" (failed before) |
| AI-4 | High | `list_tasks`, `update_task` and `add_task` reached todos of spaces outside the agent's grant | Fixed `7548762b` | The task verbs never asked the grant; `add_task` used the open space even when not reached | `reachesSpace(call, id)`; a limited agent sees, changes and adds only todos of its spaces | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "a todo of a space the agent does not reach…", "a todo is not added into an open space the agent does not reach" (failed before) |
| AI-5 | High | Joining the AI key vault overwrote a different local account (and its API key) that had the same generated id, e.g. `compatible-1` Ollama vs OpenRouter, or two `anthropic-2` added offline | Fixed `87a22209` | Merge by id with `theirs ?? mine` where the base does not hold the id; ids are positional | `apart`: a local account that is not alike the remote one under its id moves to a fresh id with its key, seen and prints before the merge | `vault/merge.test.ts` 3 tests (2 failed before); vault, accounts, store tests passed |
| AI-6 | High | On Windows `Private./Diary.md` got past a lock on `Private` for agents (Rust and Win32 open it as `Private\Diary.md`) | Fixed `b8a66db7` | JS path comparison lacked the Win32 trailing dot and space rule Rust's `win32_name` applies | `space-paths.ts` folds names as `win32_name` does when the platform is Windows | `protection.test.ts` "a path Windows reads as a protected one", `space-paths.test.ts` "a name Windows ends with a dot or a space" (failed before); not run on a real Windows file system |
| AI-7 | Medium | `add_row` refused every base at the top of a space that has no folder filter | Fixed `6ab4ca57` | `judgedForWriting('')` throws; the `'.'` handling after it was unreachable | An empty or `.` folder is the top of the space | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "a base at the top of a space takes a row at the top of the space" (failed before) |

| AI-9 | Medium | `workspace_tabs` `move` and `arrange` answered with every tab's title, including tabs of spaces the agent does not reach | Fixed `1eeb8545` | They returned `workspace.tabs` rather than `tabsInReach` | `tabsInReach(call)` | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "are not listed in the answer to a move" (failed before) |
| AI-10 | Low | `read_setting`/`write_setting` with `constructor` or `toString` threw a TypeError | Fixed `1eeb8545` | `SETTINGS[key]` and `in` read inherited keys | `Object.hasOwn` | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "a key an object holds by inheritance is no setting" (failed before) |
| AI-11 | Low | If the agents port was not up on the first stop watch, the stop button never stopped `edit_rows` or `ask_reader` | Fixed `1eeb8545` | `heedStops` cached a failed hear forever | A failed hear is tried again on the next call | By reading; agent-verbs tests passed |
| AI-12 | Low | A one-shot AI completion lost its last `data:` line when the stream did not end with a line break | Fixed `3aab0d98` | `streamed()` broke on `done` before flushing the decoder and the held line | Flush on `done`, as `sseEvents` does | `complete.test.ts` "keeps its last line, though no line break follows it" (failed before) |

Limit: a note restored from the account's Recently deleted on another device does not get its lock back |
