| SO51 | Medium (security) | Re-audit round 14: protected notes and folders, and the protection password, set in one window were not known in another: there the items showed unlocked and open to agents, the next protect or unprotect wrote the stale set over them (also to the account for that space), and protecting with a password asked for a new one and replaced the first | Fixed `8b5c5aa2` | `protection` read `orden:protected` and the password once at load and wrote its whole map from memory | It hears its three keys from other windows and takes what they wrote | `protect/src/windows.effect.test.ts` (failed before) |
| SO52 | Medium (security) | Re-audit round 14: "Lock again after" (Settings > General) shortened in one window left the other windows' unlocked items open by the old time, or until Orden closed | Fixed `8b5c5aa2`, `e3b85cab`, `b82ee485` | `unlocks.relock` was read once at load, and a timer armed while away kept its old length; SO49's Pass relock had the same armed-timer gap | Note protection hears its relock key, restarts its grace and, while away, its timer by the new time; Pass re-arms its timer when a heard relock time differs | `protect/src/windows.effect.test.ts` "relocks this window, already away", `relock.test.ts` "a relock time changed while away" (failed before); protect, pass-sync, crash-reports, sharing protect, settings 445 passed; svelte-check 0 errors |
| SO53 | Low | Re-audit round 14, from SO46: a stopped crash watch that was sending kept hearing other windows | Fixed `fb214b28` | Only the not-sending stop removed the `storage` listener | Both stops remove it | `watch.effect.test.ts` "a stopped watch hears no other window" |
| SO54 | Medium (privacy, data loss) | Re-audit round 15: Settings > Space showed a locked space's AGENTS.md without unlocking, and words typed there were dropped by the locked write but taken as saved, so they were gone on the next open | Fixed `4c5f8dd7` | The pane read the file with no lock check, and `writeNoteText` gave no word when `replaceInNotes` refused a locked path | A locked file reads as unreadable (empty and read-only) until it is unlocked; `writeNoteText` says whether it wrote and the field stays owed when it did not | `SpacePane.effect.test.ts` "a locked space", `instructions-field.test.ts` "a write the file refuses" (failed before) |
| SO55 | Medium (security) | Re-audit round 15, from SO52: choosing "Right away" (or a shorter time) in this window left an unlock's grace running, so locked items kept opening without asking until it ran out | Fixed `77595ae6` | `setRelock` only stored the value; SO52's retiming ran only for other windows | Setting the time here retimes the grace and the away timer the same way | `protect/src/windows.effect.test.ts` "relocking right away, chosen in this window" (failed before); settings, protect, workspace, views, pane-tree 1541 passed; svelte-check 0 errors |

Left as Low, each with a way out or a rare trigger:
- After a relaunch mid-setup, Back is gone for the first screen shown, and the guide no longer knows what was imported (in-memory trail and `brought`).
- Setup's generic "Use an API key" field ignores a key it cannot place, and reads OpenRouter's `sk-or-` keys as OpenAI's; Other lists those providers by name.
- Back during a browser or notes import in setup, then forward, can start the same import twice.
- Answering "Do you write code?" twice keeps the markdown display of the first answer.
- App shortcuts still run under the setup and the guide (Escape or a click recovers).
- Typing a refused key in setup for a provider already connected writes over that account's good key; moving the wallpaper blur and switching theme within 400 ms remakes at the default blur; a signed-in reader launching offline with no spaces is shown setup; `file:` theme names over 64 characters never sync.
- A refused settings save (only reachable through an accepted Low) drops every field owed with the bad one; at launch the look's two reads can wear the older look for a moment until our own save's hub event puts ours back.
- Settings search lists shortcuts that are hidden, and aliases twice; slider ranges are narrower than the values the zoom shortcuts can set; at most 200 shortcut overrides are kept.
- Two quick saves of settings reaching the server out of order can leave the older value there; a glasses command word over 60 characters is kept only until a restart.
- GPU switch shows the new value if the backend refuses; a font name `familyName` rejects stays in the field though nothing is applied.
- Re-audit rounds 5 to 11 Lows: modes clears an owed field when an older save of the same value is heard after a newer save failed (two quick toggles and a drop in between); AltGr pressed alone on Linux records a bare `AltGraph`; after going Back to the AI step a sign-in that worked shows Sign in again; connecting a keyless compatible provider such as Ollama twice adds a second account; choosing a shortcut preset or Reset all turns Vim keys off without a word; the AGENTS.md field can write over an outside edit merged in while typing during a write; the setup sign-in sheet waits up to 3 s on the old session's answer when signing in over one that never answered; Put it on the bar counts hidden buttons toward the 24; on the phone the AI step is rarely skipped since synced AI accounts arrive after setup opens; picking a browser, Back and another keeps settings the second preset does not name; a regional search engine for another language reads outside its listed choices; folder theme ids read outside the Style choices until the folder is listed; export page setup changed in two windows at once loses fields; a remake already running when a picture from another window is heard can write over it.
- Re-audit round 12 Lows: single-value preferences (tab cycling, back swipe, unloading tabs, icon and fitted tabs, tab preview delay, web memory saver, quit warning, tray, update channel, quick add choices, archive list) and whole-record view preferences (explorer sort and view, essential letters, glass grounds, password generator) changed in one window show their old value in another until a relaunch, and that window's next change of the same record writes it back; usage tallies can still roll in a window that hears an opt-out only before its usage watch starts (about a second after launch); pressing Enter within 600 ms of pasting an API key in setup can save it twice, and with a bad key the second save can leave the key in the keychain.
- Re-audit round 14 Lows: a crash question still showing in a second window keeps its box ticked after the first window answered off, and confirming there turns reports back on; after switching Pass from PIN to password in one window, another still shows PIN entry until "Use password"; Skip on the appearance step still puts back the old look when the account's look arrives with the very scheme the reader just picked.
- Re-audit round 15 Lows: a Claude Code or Gemini sign-in in setup that is never finished keeps its row spinning after the 5 minute watch gives up (Skip still works) and keeps the account it made; an item moved in another window shows locked here until unlocked again; `protection.said` marks a later set as sent when an older push lands after a newer one failed.
- Re-audit round 16 Lows: the command palette and an agent's `write_setting` can turn on a locked switch (an Experimental feature while Experimental is off; it does nothing until Experimental is on); words typed in a space's AGENTS.md can be dropped when "Lock again after: Right away" locks during the blur write; an agent can set the glass opacity slider below its live minimum; the browser import step has no catch of its own (its runner catches today).
- A file theme shows Classic for a moment at launch until the theme folder is read; on macOS a one-variant theme leaves the native window in the system scheme (a fix needs a system scheme source the window does not change).

### Re-audit round 16 (2026-11-15, at `6f9a2228`)

A complete re-audit of the area (settings panes and every store they read and write, the agent `write_setting` path, setup and the guide, themes, shortcuts, strings) and of the round 15 fixes found no Critical, High or Medium bug. Its Lows are listed above. This is what the audit checked, not a claim that the area is free of bugs.

### Full suite (2026-11-15, at `51b42214`)

`pnpm -r --workspace-concurrency=1 test` on Node 24 ran every package to the end:

- Passed: autocomplete 1, bases 1050, chats 295, contracts 37, editor 2070, glasses 235, lang-html 3, markdown 1903, online 334, rooms 72, scan 492, sync-core 352, services/sync 3063.
- apps/desktop: 1240 files passed, 3 failed (9940 tests passed, 2 failed). All three came from this branch and are fixed in SO44. Each one, and every module SO44 touched, passes on its own.
- packages/pass `ssh.test.ts` needs `ssh-keygen`, which this container lacks. services/machine's fork-bomb test fails here, and in a later run it took the container down with it. This branch touches neither.
- The whole suite was not run again after SO44, because the container cannot take a second full run. The SO44 changes were checked file by file, and svelte-check reports 0 errors.

## Web app and PWA

Started 2026-11-15 on `claude/project-thread-xe1yxs`, from `unstable`. Scope: the web
backend in the browser, the service worker, offline, storage and install, the published
site's own script, and phone layouts. Same method and severities as above.

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| W1 | Medium | On a phone, tapping a note in a published page's graph did nothing | Fixed | The click opened the `hovered` node, which only `pointermove` sets; a tap sends no move, and `pointerleave` clears it before the click | The click asks which node is under its own point | `site.effect.test.ts` (failed before); Playwright on a Pixel 7 profile: a tap stayed on `/a` before, opens `/b` after; sync `site-script`, `site`, `graph` 94 passed |
| W2 | Medium | In the web app, a search typed while a large space was still being read left most notes out of every later search (or listed them twice), until the space was let go | Fixed | A cold search's `walk` replaced the shared `order` with its own list, stopped at the hit limit, and the warm pass then marked the space whole over that prefix; two walks at once pushed onto one array | A walk collects into its own list and only publishes it, merged and sorted with what is listed, when it read the whole space | `space-cache.test.ts` "a walk that stops early while the warm pass reads…" (`of: 1` before) and "two walks at once…" (`of: 6` before); web-backend and search 280 passed |
| W3 | High | Web app: edits an older-version tab made in the former store after a split were dropped when it was carried again, and its snapshots that shared a number with one in `orden` were lost | Fixed | The carry put a row only where `orden` had none, so an older tab's later edit lost to the copy carried before it, and an autoincrement snapshot under a taken number was skipped | The row changed later (by `modified`) wins; a snapshot under a number taken by another is added beside it under a new number, once | `database-former.test.ts` "takes what the older store changed later…" (failed before; also run twice to show the snapshot is added once) |
| W4 | Medium | Web app: a carry from the former store that failed (full storage, another tab holding `orden`) left the app on an empty `orden` | Fixed | `databaseName` swallowed the failure and returned `orden` | `carryDatabase` says whether it carried everything; if not, the app stays on the former store, where the notes are | `database-former.test.ts` "a carry that does not finish…" (`stuck-to` before); web-backend 237 passed |
| W5 | Critical | A kept space (`.orden/<Name>`) whose working folder was gone but whose `.work.json` record was left (trashed and restored, the folder deleted by hand, or a rename of it that failed) had every note deleted from the store, with no trash, the next time a terminal, Reveal, export or a link reached the folder | Fixed | `keep()` made an empty working folder and the mirror still read the old record, so every file it remembered and did not find there read as deleted in the terminal | The mirror makes the folder itself and, when it was not there, starts from no record, so the store fills it again | `work.test.ts` "a working folder that is gone, its record left behind…" (notes deleted before); web-backend internal 57 passed |
| W6 | High | Moving or copying a note from a folder space into a kept space wrote over a note there whose name differed only in case (macOS, Windows) or Unicode spelling, and the move then deleted the source | Fixed | `across` asked `what(to)`, which compares stored names with `===`, while the kept folder folds case and NFC | `across` also asks the kept folder itself, which folds names, about the target and its PDF companions | `spaces.test.ts` "is refused where a note of the same name, spelled another way, already lives" (resolved before) |
| W7 | Low | Moving a kept folder named in another Unicode spelling (NFD for an NFC name) put its files beside the new folder (`BistroMenu.md`) | Fixed | `under` cut each child's path by the length of the caller's spelling | Children keep the segments after the folder's depth | `folder.test.ts` "moves a folder named in another Unicode spelling…" (failed before); internal 59 passed |
| W8 | Medium | In a kept space, a save that landed while its note was renamed left the old name on the same stored file: deleting the old name later deleted the renamed note's words (likewise after a trash or delete) | Fixed | `write` read the entry before awaiting the bytes and then set it back under the old name, unchecked | After the bytes are written, the entry is looked up by its key; it is updated where it now lives, and nothing is put back if it went | `folder.test.ts` "a write that lands while its note is renamed…" (old name still there before); web-backend 241 passed |
| W9 | Medium | Web app: two deletes at once (two tabs, or a sync pass beside a delete or a restore) kept only one entry in Recently deleted; the other note sat under `/.trash` for good, never listed, restored or purged. Two tabs could also give two items one id | Fixed | Every change to the trash list read `meta.trash`, awaited, and wrote the whole list back; ids were a time and a per-tab counter | Each change to the list runs in turn, within a tab and across tabs (Web Locks), on the list as it then is; ids carry a random part | `commands.test.ts` "two deletes at once both land in Recently deleted…" (one entry before); web-backend 242 passed |
| W10 | Medium | Web app with two tabs (or a file opened on its own at `/asset/…`): pressing a notice brought forward whichever window the browser listed first, which knew nothing of it, so the press did nothing | Fixed | The worker took `matchAll()[0]`; only the tab that showed a notice holds it | The worker leaves `/asset/` windows out and asks the app's windows which one showed the notice (300 ms), then focuses that one, else the first window of the app; a tab answers from the notices it showed | `sw.test.ts` "goes to the window that showed it…", "goes to the first window of the app…" (failed before); notify contract "tell the worker which window showed them…" (failed before); 31 passed |
| W11 | High | Web app ([#1252](https://github.com/lxorb/orden/issues/1252)): offline, a reload showed the browser's error page instead of the notes kept on the device, and any part of the app not loaded yet failed | Fixed in part (whole-app download waits on David's answer) | The service worker answered only `/asset/…`; the page and the app's own code were left to the network | The worker keeps the page (network first, kept copy offline) and every built file under `/assets/` it serves (kept first), for the last three releases; on start it keeps the page and the files it names, and the page hands it the files it loaded before the worker took over | `sw-offline.test.ts` (5, failed before); Playwright on the built app, reload offline: before `ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED`, after the welcome note in the editor; platform and web-backend 670 passed. A part never opened before still needs the network |
| W12 | Medium | Renaming or deleting a kept space while a save into it was on its way failed that save (`BroadcastChannel is closed`) after writing its bytes under the old folder's name, which a later start could list as a space of its own | Fixed | `renameKept` and `removeKept` flushed and then moved the folder, with nothing holding back a write already started or one that came after | The folder is retired first: new writes are refused, the ones on their way finish and are written, then the folder moves; a rename that fails mounts it again | `spaces.test.ts` "renamed while a save is on its way…" (threw before); web-backend 250 passed |
| W13 | Medium | Two windows on one kept space: a change made in one just after the other's (before word of it arrived) wrote its index over it, so a note just made in the other window vanished and its file was left unlisted | Fixed | `change()` reloaded only when a BroadcastChannel message had already said the index moved; the lock can be granted before that message lands | Each window notes the newest index it wrote where every window reads it at once (localStorage beside the lock); a change under the lock reads the index again when its own is older | `folder.test.ts` "a window that takes its turn before word of the last change reaches it…" (note lost before); web-backend 251 passed |
| W14 | Medium | Every start of the web app, and the first search in its worker, waited 400 ms before reading a single note, though the former store had long been carried and deleted | Fixed | `databaseName` asked for an older tab and waited out the answer before looking whether the former store was there at all | It looks first; with no former store it opens `orden` at once | `database-former-later.test.ts` "with no older store at all, no older tab is waited for" (403 ms before); web-backend database tests 9 passed |
| W15 | Low | Web app, first visit: a sound or film given by `<source>` that asked for its file before the asset worker took over stayed broken | Fixed | On `controllerchange` the page set `src` on the `<source>` again, which does nothing until its media element is loaded again | The media element around a `<source>` is loaded again | `serve-assets.effect.test.ts` (failed before) |
| W16 | Low | Moving a note that is not UTF-8 (a Latin-1 `.md`) from a folder space into a kept space stored it with its letters replaced by `�`, and deleted the original | Fixed | `write_bytes` into the kept store reads a text file through a lenient `TextDecoder` | `across` checks every text file it carries into a kept space first and refuses the move, leaving everything where it was, if one is not UTF-8 (as the desktop's `read_note` refuses it) | `spaces.test.ts` "that is not UTF-8 text is refused…" (moved and mangled before); web-backend 253 passed |
| W17 | Low | Moving a folder from a folder space into a kept space deleted, for good, the files `read_tree` did not list (past its depth of 32) | Fixed | The copy went by the listing, the delete by `delete_folder`, which removes the whole tree | The source loses only the files that were carried, then the folders that are left empty; the rest stays where it was | `spaces.test.ts` "in a folder deeper than the listing reaches…" (deleted before); web-backend 254 passed |
| W18 | Low | Carrying the former web store read every value of the new one back into memory only to compare keys, about twice the store at once, which can run a tab with many pictures out of memory and repeat at every start | Fixed | `holdsAll` called `dump(to)`, which reads values with the keys | It lists the new store's keys alone | `database-former.test.ts` "lists what the new store holds by key…" (`dump('read-to')` before) |
| W19 | Medium | On a phone, the card a long press on a link opens on a published page vanished as the finger lifted, so it could not be read; a touch held on any link also opened it | Fixed | Touch pointers drove the mouse's hover: `pointerover` started the card, and `pointerout`, which a touch sends on lifting, hid it | Hover follows the mouse and pen alone; on touch the card comes from the long press and stays until a tap outside it or a scroll | `site.effect.test.ts` "a note's card, opened by a long press…" (hidden before); sync `site-script`, `site` passed |
| W20 | Medium | On a phone, once the Scratchpad was opened, tapping a note in the list or in search left the Scratchpad over the screen, and a new note's words went into the Scratchpad; this came back after a reload | Fixed | Opening a note on a drawer screen only shut the left drawer; the Scratchpad, full width on a phone, is the right side, and shutting the left drawer kept it covered | Opening a note on a drawer screen gets both sides out of its way: the left drawer, and the right one with the Scratchpad over it | `workspace.test.ts` "on a phone, a note opened from the drawer…" (Scratchpad still on before) and "beside a wide window…" (left as it was); Playwright on Pixel 7 and iPhone 13: "Read me" opens and new-note typing lands in the note |
| W21 | Medium | On a phone, tapping a heading in Outline or an entry in Links moved the note behind the right drawer, which stayed open over it | Fixed | Landing on a line shut `closePanel()`, whose side is the left one, and Outline and Links live on the right on a phone | Landing on a line and opening a linked note both use the same step as W20, which shuts both sides | Playwright on Pixel 7 and iPhone 13: after tapping "Section 3" and a backlink the right drawer is shut (`open` before); workspace 365 passed |
| W22 | Medium | Web app, found by the final re-audits and brought in by W4: a tab left on the former store by a carry that did not finish never said so, so the next carry that finished (another tab, or the tab's own search worker) deleted the former store under it; and once it did say so, a new tab or its own search worker still opened the empty or partial `orden` beside it | Fixed | Only the older-tab path answered on the windows channel, and the answer did not say which store the tab stays on, so a new realm took "`orden` exists" as leave to use it | A tab that stays on an older store, after a failed carry or beside an older tab, answers with the stores it stays on; a new tab or worker that hears its own older store named joins it there, and nothing deletes it while that tab is open. The answer is kept per store, so the notes and sync stores do not steer each other; an answer naming only other stores is not taken for an older build; a realm that stayed keeps staying when it asks again (the sync store asks at every restart) | `database-former-later.test.ts` "a tab left on the older store by a carry that did not finish…" (the former store deleted before, then the next tab on `orden`) "a tab staying on one older store does not keep a new tab on another…", "a tab that stayed… stays there when it asks again…" and "an answer about some other older store…" (both failed before); re-audit's own repros (blocked and quota-full carries) now give the older store to the next realm; web-backend and sync-client 685 passed |

Low issues found and left as they are:

| # | Severity | Issue | Why left |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WL1 | Low | A conversion to a kept space that fails after the new folder is mounted (`convert.ts`) deletes that folder while it is still mounted, and the original folder stays hidden until the app starts again | Needs a throw from `fileMoved` or `rehome_snapshots`; the source and its backup are kept, so nothing is lost, and a restart shows the space again |
| WL2 | Low | `mountOf` in `roots.ts` routes a path by its first segments without resolving `..`, so a path like `/Plans/../x.md` would be sent to the kept store | No caller passes a path that is not already normalised |
| WL3 | Low | After a carry from the former store that did not finish (W4), the app stays on the former store, but the asset worker reads only `orden` (its read of the former store went with #1676), so a picture or file kept only in the former store does not show, and one changed there afterwards shows its older carried copy | Needs a carry that failed; the next start whose carry finishes brings every file over and ends it |
| WL4 | Low | On a phone, "Move to the right" in the Search menu shows Search twice and leaves a state that comes back after a reload | A phone keeps its right side to Outline and Links, so the move half lands; it needs the menu item that should not be offered there, and the next move back undoes it |
| WL5 | Low | In landscape on a phone with a notch, sheets, the context menu's title and the right drawer's rows run past the left and right safe edges | Only with a notch and the phone turned; the text stays readable and nothing is cut off. Tested with insets set by hand, as Playwright cannot emulate them |
| WL6 | Low | On a touch screen, a tap on a search result or an Outline heading puts the caret in the note, which on Android may bring up the keyboard | The caret at the found line is meant; not confirmed on a real device |
| WL7 | Low | On a phone, the drawer's "+" button covers the right end of the last file row when the list is scrolled to its end, and the search "Replace 0" label sits high on an iPhone SE | Looks only; every row can still be reached |
| WL8 | Low | The asset worker counts every same-origin HTML page it is sent to (a pass link, the feedback status page) as a release, so three such visits drop the app's kept code, and it keeps those pages even when they say `no-store` | The browser's own cache of the hashed files usually still serves them offline; the app's page itself stays kept |
| WL9 | Low | During the first load of a new release, its code can be kept under the previous release and dropped one release early | Same exposure as WL8, one release later |
| WL10 | Low | The cache for code kept before the first release was known can be made again after it was carried, and is then never dropped | A few files of storage |
| WL11 | Low | A notice pressed when no tab answers goes to the first window of the app, which can be a pass link or feedback window | No worse than before W10 |
| WL12 | Low | On a touchscreen laptop that reports a mouse, a touch on a published page's link no longer opens its card | The card is a preview; the link still opens on a tap |
| WL13 | Low | A film or sound from `/asset/` given by `<source>` starts again when a new asset worker takes over | Happens once per release of the worker, the moment it takes over |
| WL14 | Low | A tab staying on the former store that is frozen or busy past the 400 ms answer window can have the former store carried and deleted under it; it then reopens an empty former store until reloaded (its notes are in `orden`) | As before this branch; needs a failed carry and then a suspended tab |
| WL15 | Low | Startup's first look at whether the former store is there waits behind a delete of the former store that another tab's open connection is blocking | Only during the one start that carries, while an older tab is closing |
| WL16 | Low | When the carry's upgrade of `orden` is blocked, its open request stays pending and the connection it opens later is never closed | Needs the two stores at different versions, which they never are today |
| WL17 | Low | A realm whose carry fails just after another realm's carry finished and deleted the former store falls back to the former store, which then opens empty, and later tabs join it there until it closes | Needs one carry to fail while another at the same moment succeeds; no trigger found at today's store versions |
| WL18 | Low | Two sync store openings at once in one tab: if one's carry fails, the other's in-use check does not hear it and can delete the older sync store | Needs a failed carry during a sync restart |
| WL19 | Low | A realm remembers that nobody answered, so a later sync store opening carries a kept the former sync store again without asking, which can bring back outbox rows already sent | As on `unstable` before this branch; needs an older tab that kept the former sync store |

Final re-audit (2026-11-15): four independent passes. The first found W22, brought in by the W4 fix. The second and third each found a gap left in the W22 fix, and both are fixed. The fourth, on the older-store handover as it now stands, found nothing at Medium or above, and the second pass found nothing at Medium or above in the rest of the branch. Totals for this section: Critical 1 fixed, High 3 fixed, Medium 13 fixed, Low 5 fixed and 19 left as listed. This is what the audits checked, not a claim that the area is free of bugs.

## Sync and server

Burn-down of 2026-11-15, branch `claude/project-thread-ctlhxr` from `unstable`. Area: sync (sync-core, the v2 server, the desktop sync client and its conflict handling), the server's other services (rooms, sharing, sites, blobs, inbox, machines, feedback), accounts, sign-in mail and account storage. Rows are numbered S1 and on so they do not collide with the other threads of this burn-down.

Method: baseline on Node 24 (sync service 3063 passed and 1 expected fail, sync-core 351, online 334); a read-only audit of each part, each finding traced through the code; then for each bug a failing regression test, the root cause, the fix, and the touched suites, `tsc`, eslint and prettier again.

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Medium | After the owner deleted a shared space, every open note room socket of a signed-in member (and the owner's other devices) kept reading and writing that space's notes | Fixed `8833d4d5` | `ACCESS` in `rooms/access.ts` never looked at `spaces.deleted`, so `revoke` and the minute check still answered `write`; the #456 test opened sockets with no login, which skips that query | `case when sp.deleted = 1 then 'none'` first | `rooms.test.ts` "has every signed-in socket closed when the owner deletes the space", "closes a signed-in socket within a minute once the space is gone" (both failed before); rooms, room-state, sync2-rooms, share, guests 538 passed |

| S16 | High | A mailed invitation link signed whoever followed it into the invited address's whole account for 30 days, with no code typed: a forwarded mail, or a guest who had only typed that address on an approval link, got the account; the mail said a code would be sent, and none was | Fixed `443da479` | `redeemInvitation` called `signingIn` and opened a session for the invited address; the invite token was taken as proof of the address | Following an invitation signed out mails the sign-in code to the invited address and answers `{ code, email }`; the app opens its own code step for that address and walks the link in as the account once the code is typed (David chose this, 2026-11-15) | `guests.test.ts` "the link in an invitation" (10 tests), "keeps what it had when the invitation is to the address it said it was at", `share.test.ts` "opens no sign-in by itself…", `second.test.ts` "on an invitation, once the mailed code is typed", `joining.test.ts` "a mailed invitation" (5 tests); e2e `share.py` updated to type the code (not run here) |
| S17 | Medium | A todo changed through the connector (`add_task`, `update_task`) silently lost to what a device whose clock ran ahead had written to the same field, while the tool said it was done (found by the to-do burn-down) | Fixed `f50b6394` | `keep` stamped every field with the server's clock, which loses to a later stamp from a device ahead of it | Each field is stamped just past the stamp the account already holds for it when that one is later than now | `mcp-tasks.test.ts` "a change lands over what a device with a clock ahead wrote…" (failed before) |
| S18 | Low | A connector change that made a todo larger than a device reads (8000 three-byte characters of description) answered success but changed nothing | Fixed `f50b6394` | S4 made the server skip such a merge instead of losing the todo, but the connector was not told | `keep` measures the merged todo first and refuses in a sentence | `mcp-tasks.test.ts` "a change that would make a todo larger…" (failed before); mcp-tasks, inbox, mcp-protected 31 passed |

### Known and left as Low

- An inbox change that would make one to-do larger than a device reads is not stored and the sender is not told; its change stays on that device only (before S4 the to-do vanished everywhere). Telling the sender needs a refusal in the inbox protocol and UI.
- Blob dedup and `letGo` still race in a window of one round trip: a row can land between `letGo`'s check and its delete. S5 closes the common case.
- The v2 feed and MCP `list_notes` show an agent the names, sizes and metadata of protected notes, never their words.
- A site's pages may cost up to 100 queries in a space of 50,000 notes.
- A one-note site is turned off by an atomic delete and create at its name, and restoring the note does not turn it back on.
- Room sockets reach a note shared on its own after it was trashed, unlike HTTP.
- `purgeSpace` does not check again whether the space was restored meanwhile; a large space restored before the nightly run is partly emptied.
- The desktop client lets go of a note's file a moment after checking its hash; a save by another program in that gap is lost.
- Sign-in and OAuth: a guest is claimed before the second factor, OAuth `redirect_uri` is optional, a stale refresh token revokes the grant, ending other sessions leaves OAuth grants, and IDNA/NFC spellings of one address make two accounts.
- Merge edges: plane diverge edges, CRLF in `withBlocksOf`, order positions past 128.
- A block comment in `sync-server-accounts/src/auth.ts` (inside `accountFor`'s catch) and a few others predate this burn-down and go against the no-comments rule.

### Tests

The full suite (`pnpm -r test`, one package at a time, Node 24) passed apart from two tests that cannot pass in this container: `@orden/pass` "is read by ssh-keygen as the same key" (no `ssh-keygen` here) and `@orden/machine` "a fork bomb is held at the process limit" (the container runs as root, so the process limit does not hold; the run took the container down). Neither package is touched by this branch.

## CLI, MCP and importers

Started 2026-11-15 on `claude/project-thread-jzf39m`, from `unstable`. Scope: the `orden`
CLI and its automation endpoint, the desktop and hosted MCP servers, the migrator and every
importer, and the shared packages bases, contracts, markdown, scan, pass, autocomplete,
lang-html and themes. Seven read-only audits ran in parallel; each finding came with a
failing reproduction before it was kept. Each fix starts from a failing regression test.

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |

### Re-audit 1 (2026-11-15)

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C46 | Medium | Regression from C41: a dead `./` or `../` link resolved to a note of the same name elsewhere, counted as its backlink and was rewritten on its rename | Fixed `e531b71c` | Shrinking C41 to the startup budget dropped its guard for explicitly relative links | A link written as `./` or `../` resolves as before C41, never from the root or by name | `wikilink.test.ts`, `link-index.test.ts` "a relative link to a note that is not there" (failed before); editor 2073 passed |
| C47 | Medium | Regression from C3: hosted `list_backlinks` missed links written from the space root or by name alone | Fixed `3f9752dd` | The new resolver tried only the note's folder | It follows the app's order: relative, root, nearest by name, with `./` and `../` kept relative | `mcp.test.ts` "markdown links the app reads from the top and by name" (failed before) |
| C48 | Medium | Sibling of C1: hosted `list_notes` and `list_backlinks` stopped at 1000 notes without saying so | Fixed `3f9752dd` | The same silent limit | They read one past the limit and say when they stopped | `mcp.test.ts` "lists past the most read" (failed before); hosted MCP 73 passed |
| C49 | Medium | Sibling of C19: `read_terminal` on an online terminal still connecting lost its answer after 30 s | Fixed `9d379b1e` | `declared_wait` gave it no wait, while it waits up to 5 s plus 60 s | It is given the terminal start wait | `endpoint` and `mcp::link` Rust tests (failed before) |
| C50 | Medium | An edit waiting on a typing reader was applied, or refused, after the endpoint had already reported failure; a retrying agent inserted twice | Fixed `e368889c` | The 30 s edit patience was checked after a try and then waited another quiet, and queued time was not counted | Patience is 20 s and counts from before the queue; a Rust test ties the TS constants to the endpoint's | 2 `edit.test.ts` tests (failed before); `cargo test --lib` 1900 passed, 1 root-only failure unrelated |
| C51 | Low | With no window listening, a long verb waited its whole extended patience (up to 720 s) | Fixed `7d225b2c` | The listener wait took the verb's full deadline | Waiting for a listener is capped at the base 30 s | `endpoint` Rust test (failed before) |
| C52 | Low | An agent's `limit: null` (or `wait_ms`, `lines`) read as 1 | Fixed `7b9b668f` | `Number(null)` is 0, clamped to 1 | Null reads as left out | `call.test.ts` (failed before) |
| C53 | Medium | Regressions from C24: the hosted `query_base` read dates with an offset on the server's clock, and a timeline drag or cell edit wrote them back as local time without their offset | Fixed `7e5eb440` | C24 turned a zoned date into a plain local one at read time, on the process clock, and dropped the offset | A date keeps its written wall clock and offset; the reader's zone comes from the context (local on desktop, the user's on hosted), and every instant-based operation honours it; edits move the written value and keep its offset | `mcp-tasks.test.ts` "a date written with its offset is read on the clock of the person asking", `timeline.test.ts`, `dates.test.ts`, `write.test.ts` (failed before); bases 1060, views and rows 177, hosted MCP 93 passed under UTC, Los Angeles and Tokyo |
| C54 | Medium | A timeline drag of `due: 2026-03-10T23:00` (no seconds) wrote `2026-03-11T23`, no longer a date | Fixed `58daf118` | `timeIn` dropped a trailing `:00` meant as seconds from an `HH:MM` time | It drops `:00` only after `HH:MM` | `timeline.test.ts` "without an offset or seconds keeps its hour and minutes as written" (failed before) |

| C108 | Medium | A Notion relation to a page named with brackets was not read as a relation, which turned the whole column into raw text with paths in every row | Fixed `4315d237` | The relation pattern had the same bracket-refusing target | Same balanced-bracket target | `notion-database.test.ts` "is the page named with brackets" (failed before) |

| C156 | Medium | An agent could capture the live page of a reader web tab whose `.url` web note sits in a folder kept from agents, and the tab lists and the reader-tab list given to the browser verbs named its address | Fixed `c4cfb411` | Reader web tabs are reached by tab id, and the capture, the tab rows and `readerTabs` never asked the protection | `capture_to_note` refuses such a tab as `read_note` would, tab rows leave out its address, and `readerTabs` leaves the tab out | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "a reader tab whose web note is kept from agents is neither captured nor named by address" (failed before); agent-verbs 128, agent-host, weight and no-comments 238 passed |

### Re-audit 28 (2026-11-16)

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C157 | Medium | C156 incomplete: an agent granted every space (the default) could still read, screenshot, find in and drive a reader web tab kept from agents by its id through the browser verbs | Fixed `8c5a510b` | The Rust `place()` checked a reader tab against the window's reader-tab list only for agents with named spaces, and `lendTab` lent any web tab | `place()` checks every agent caller against the reader-tab list (which C156 cleared of locked tabs), and `lendTab` lends no tab kept from agents | `agent-verbs.test.ts` "a reader tab whose web note is kept from agents is neither captured nor named by address" now also lends nothing (failed before); agent-verbs and agent-host 164, Rust `agents::browser` tests, weight and no-comments passed |
| C158 | Medium | Picking a completion while mending a written link broke it: `[[Pla\|the plan]]` gave `[[Plan]]\|the plan]]`, `[[Pla#Head]]` gave `[[Plan]]#Head]]` | Fixed `5351359f` | The completion replaced from `[[` to the caret and stepped only over a `]]` right at the caret, leaving the rest of the link after a closed one | The rest of the written link up to its `]]` is replaced too, and its heading and shown words are carried into the link written | `complete.test.ts` "picking a row while mending a link already written" (3 of 4 failed before); editor 2089 passed |

### Re-audit 29 (2026-11-16)

Both auditors (importers and links; CLI, MCP and shared packages) found no Critical, High or Medium bug. C157 and C158 were reviewed and hold. Lows recorded: a table-escaped `\|` alias written back as a raw `|` when a link in a table cell is mended (the cell was broken before C158 too); `slugify` not reaching GitHub-style `#hello--world` or `#intro-1` anchors (unconfirmed); `workspace_tabs open` with `view-source:` of a site denied to the agent opening a reader tab (the browser verbs still refuse it); hosted `add_task`/`update_task` skipping the desktop label limits and not collapsing inner spaces.

### Merged with unstable (2026-11-17)

Where unstable had fixed the same bug its way, the landed design was kept: protected notes shut to programs at the route (`programShut`) in place of C147 and C149's own checks, the hosted chats filter for C151, agent to-do spaces (`reachesSpace`, `namedSpace`, `openSpace`) from #2144 and #2145, `inboxFits` for hosted to-do sizes, the landed `exportsOnlyWhatAgentsMay`, `agentsMayAll` and `spelled` path names. Kept from this branch on top, each with its test: the link completion that holds typed words (C148, C150; unstable's `shiftSince` still dropped them), a capture folder judged before the page is captured, `set_task` matching tasks as `edit_note` does and keeping the call's deadline, a to-do added from an AI panel thread landing in the thread's space, `list_tasks` listing to-dos that start later or were done another day, and an export refused while any note in the space is kept from agents (an export can embed them). A single-date note bar's edges move nothing, as unstable has it.

The full suite after the merge (Node 24) found three fixes the merge had undone, put back with their tests: hosted locks fold names letter by letter again (C112, `D\u0131ary` for `Diary`), hosted `read_chat`/`search_chats` read a null limit as left out and search on the asker's clock, and, new with the landed route design, a token's `/v2/spaces/:space/feed` listed the names, sizes and hashes of protected notes (Medium); it is now refused in a space that keeps notes from agents. `programs.test.ts` expects the landed not-found for a protected note's id, and the old-name filter in `cli-args.test.ts` went with the variable unstable dropped.
